  - Ch49 L4895 «Stass Allie lay facedown nearby.»
  - Ch49 L4897 «She raised herself to one knee, then sprinted to Stass Allie's side to put out the flames that had engulfed her cloak.»
  - Ch49 L4897 «Allie stirred and raised herself on the palms of her hands.»
  - Ch49 L4901 «A wave of intense heat washed over her and Allie, and a hail of fragments clanged and clattered down around her.»
  - Ch50 L4965 «"Why not wait until Shaak Ti and Stass Allie arrive."»
  - Ch51 L4999 «Stass Allie, her face and hands blistered and her robes singed, was kneeling by the bodies of the four Jedi Knights with whom Mace had spoken briefly during the mag-lev evacuation.»

#### 5.8.4 Saesee Tiin (*Saesee Tiin, 19 BBY*)
- **Role:** a Council member (L2567) and the Order's ace pilot. Anakin's daydream: «But he would have been able to fly rings around the finest of Jedi pilots, including Saesee Tiin.» (L823). In the space battle he is among those who «had come closest to incapacitating the shuttle» (L5121), and Grievous drives them off by targeting innocents.
- **Scaling implications:** a piloting benchmark only. Anakin's self-assessment puts himself above Tiin.
- **Complete quote register (7 lines: every sentence that names Saesee Tiin, verbatim, in book order):**
  - Ch10 L599 «To the extent that friendships could be forged with them, Bail had come to know a few: Jedi Masters Obi-Wan Kenobi, Yoda, Mace Windu, Saesee Tiin - - the privileged few who also were allowed to meet personally with Palpatine.»
  - Ch12 L823 «But he would have been able to fly rings around the finest of Jedi pilots, including Saesee Tiin.»
  - Ch27 L2567 «Around the holoprojector table sat Council members Yoda, Mace Windu, Saesee Tiin, Ki-Adi-Mundi, Shaak Ti, Stass Allie, Plo Koon, and Kit Fisto.»
  - Ch39 L4263 «"Tiin, Koon, Ki-Adi-Mundi, and some of the others are on their way up the well," Mace said.»
  - Ch43 L4515 «"Already considered that, Master Tiin has.»
  - Ch43 L4529 «Shutting his eyes to distance himself farther, he stretched out with his feelings, placing in his mind's eye Mace and Kit Fisto skimming through the deranged sky; Shaak Ti and Stass Allie hurrying toward Palpatine's quarters in 500 Republica; Saesee Tiin, Agen Kolar, Bultar Swan, and other Jedi Masters and Jedi Knights streaking from Coruscant's envelope in their starfighters, local space flashing with energy bolts and globular explosions, ships too numerous to count embroiled in a monumental battle...»
  - Ch53 L5121 «Recalled from the battle outside the well, Agen Kolar, Saesee Tiin, and Pablo-Jill had come closest to incapacitating the shuttle, but, twice now, Grievous had managed to evade them by bringing the shuttle's laser cannons to bear on cargo pods and strewing local space with debris.»

#### 5.8.5 Other Council and Jedi pilots: Agen Kolar, Ki-Adi-Mundi, Plo Koon, Pablo-Jill, Bultar Swan
- **Agen Kolar:** Anakin's grievance: «Master Kolar lied about Quinlan Vos going to the dark side.» (L1013). He is in the starfighter pursuit of Grievous (L5121).
- **Ki-Adi-Mundi:** a Council member (L2567, L2609). A source on Grievous: «As Mace already knew from Ki-Adi-Mundi and Shaak Ti, Grievous was well trained in the Jedi arts.» (L4813).
- **Plo Koon:** a Council member: «"The Supreme Chancellor singles out Mygeeto, Saleucami, and Felucia," Plo Koon said from beneath the mask that supplied him with life gases.» (L2607).
- **Pablo-Jill and Bultar Swan:** named in the Coruscant battle (L5121; Yoda's remote viewing, L4529).
- **Scaling implications:** none beyond the shuttle pursuit, which Grievous wins as a pilot: «Grievous was almost as adept at handling a ship as he was a lightsaber» (L5119).
- **Complete quote register (8 lines: every sentence that names Other Council members, verbatim, in book order):**
  - Ch14 L1013 «Master Kolar lied about Quinlan Vos going to the dark side.»
  - Ch27 L2567 «Around the holoprojector table sat Council members Yoda, Mace Windu, Saesee Tiin, Ki-Adi-Mundi, Shaak Ti, Stass Allie, Plo Koon, and Kit Fisto.»
  - Ch27 L2607 «"The Supreme Chancellor singles out Mygeeto, Saleucami, and Felucia," Plo Koon said from beneath the mask that supplied him with life gases.»
  - Ch27 L2609 «Ki-Adi-Mundi's elongated head made a subtle nod.»
  - Ch39 L4263 «"Tiin, Koon, Ki-Adi-Mundi, and some of the others are on their way up the well," Mace said.»
  - Ch43 L4529 «Shutting his eyes to distance himself farther, he stretched out with his feelings, placing in his mind's eye Mace and Kit Fisto skimming through the deranged sky; Shaak Ti and Stass Allie hurrying toward Palpatine's quarters in 500 Republica; Saesee Tiin, Agen Kolar, Bultar Swan, and other Jedi Masters and Jedi Knights streaking from Coruscant's envelope in their starfighters, local space flashing with energy bolts and globular explosions, ships too numerous to count embroiled in a monumental battle...»
  - Ch47 L4813 «As Mace already knew from Ki-Adi-Mundi and Shaak Ti, Grievous was well trained in the Jedi arts.»
  - Ch53 L5121 «Recalled from the battle outside the well, Agen Kolar, Saesee Tiin, and Pablo-Jill had come closest to incapacitating the shuttle, but, twice now, Grievous had managed to evade them by bringing the shuttle's laser cannons to bear on cargo pods and strewing local space with debris.»

#### 5.8.6 Quinlan Vos and Tholme (offstage)
- **Role:** the clue that leads the Jedi to The Works. «A break hadn't come until Jedi Master Tholme had recalled a detail from the debriefing of his former Padawan, Quinlan Vos.» (L3063). Vos had been «tasked with assassinating a duplicitous» Senator as part of his «covert mission to penetrate Count Dooku's inner circle of dark side apprentices» (L3063), fought «a brutal duel with Master K'Kruhk», and then met Dooku in The Works (L3063). He had «managed to track Dooku to The Works, though he apparently hadn't perceived just how deeply the dark side» ran there (L2477).
- **Scaling implications:** none (offstage).
- **Complete quote register (12 lines: every sentence that names Quinlan Vos, verbatim, in book order):**
  - Ch14 L1013 «Master Kolar lied about Quinlan Vos going to the dark side.»
  - Ch16 L1145 «Aayla Secura and the Caamasi Jedi Ylenic It'kla had taken a Techno Union defector into custody on Corellia; Quinlan Vos had gone undercover to infiltrate Dooku's circle of dark side apprentices...»
  - Ch26 L2421 «Daring Way, where it crossed Vos Gesal in upper Uscru.»
  - Ch26 L2477 «One of Dooku's previous visits had followed the assassination of a faithless Senator by Jedi Knight Quinlan Vos.»
  - Ch26 L2477 «Duped by Dooku on several occasions, Vos had managed to track Dooku to The Works, though he apparently hadn't perceived just how deeply the dark side had taken root there.»
  - Ch30 L3063 «A break hadn't come until Jedi Master Tholme had recalled a detail from the debriefing of his former Padawan, Quinlan Vos.»
  - Ch30 L3063 «As part of Vos's covert mission to penetrate Count Dooku's inner circle of dark side apprentices, Vos had been tasked with assassinating a duplicitous Senator, named Viento.»
  - Ch30 L3063 «Immediately following the assassination - - and a brutal duel with Master K'Kruhk - - Vos had met briefly with Dooku in The Works.»
  - Ch30 L3063 «There, Dooku had informed his would-be protege that Vos had been incorrect in assuming that Viento was a Sith, and had again denied that he himself answered to any Master.»
  - Ch30 L3065 «At the time, no one had paid much attention to Vos's remarks, because Vos seemed to have been seduced by the dark side and lost to the Order.»
  - Ch30 L3103 «The only explanation for Quinlan Vos not having felt it was that he, too, had gone dark.»
  - Ch33 L3523 «Not surprisingly, it turns out to have been the same one where Quinlan Vos met with Dooku last year.»

#### 5.8.7 Qui-Gon Jinn (memory and Force voice)
- **Role:** a constant reference point for Obi-Wan, Anakin and Yoda. In Yoda's meditation he becomes a voice (L4229–4231: «another key has been found»; see §4.7). Obi-Wan on him: «Qui-Gon was no stranger to what the Jedi referred to as "aggressive negotiations," which typically involved lightsabers more than diplomacy.» (L285). Anakin in the rubble: «Qui-Gon, who had died - - even though, to his young mind, Jedi weren't supposed to...» (L4833).
- **Scaling implications:** none for combat. This is the first LoE seed of the Force-ghost thread that ROTS develops.
- **Complete quote register (28 lines: every sentence that names Qui-Gon, verbatim, in book order):**
  - Ch4 L241 «Yes, just as Yoda had sensed after Qui-Gon Jinn and Obi-Wan had freed him from slavery on Tatooine and brought him to the Jedi Temple, he had a lot of anger in him.»
  - Ch5 L265 «Qui-Gon Jinn hadn't believed in baiting, Obi-Wan thought as he and the commandos rode the turbolift to the fortress's lowest level.»
  - Ch5 L265 «Baiting implied a certain amount of advance planning, and Qui-Gon had no patience for that.»
  - Ch5 L267 «Qui-Gon had had the insight to recognize it more than a decade earlier, and Obi-Wan felt duty-bound to his former Master to help Anakin fulfill his destiny.»
  - Ch5 L285 «Qui-Gon had always criticized the Council for being too authoritative, and for cultivating inflexible methods of teaching.»
  - Ch5 L285 «Qui-Gon was no stranger to what the Jedi referred to as "aggressive negotiations," which typically involved lightsabers more than diplomacy.»
  - Ch5 L285 «He recalled, as if yesterday, Dooku's taunt on Geonosis that Qui-Gon would have joined Dooku in championing the Separatist cause.»
  - Ch9 L531 «"Maybe Qui-Gon should have left you at Watto's junk shop."»
  - Ch9 L567 «Good bet it's the same one Gunray tried to use on Qui-Gon and me at Naboo."»
  - Ch11 L695 «The death of your former Master, for one - - even though avenged Qui-Gon was."»
  - Ch11 L697 «"A stern Master Dooku was, to Qui-Gon and others,"»
  - Ch11 L699 «Signs there were, all about us, long before to the Temple you came; long before Qui-Gon came.»
  - Ch11 L713 «Saw in Dooku the effect of Qui-Gon's death, Sifo-Dyas did.»
  - Ch12 L817 «Maybe Qui-Gon should have left you at Watto's junk shop.»
  - Ch12 L821 «With or without Qui-Gon's or Watto's help, he would eventually have won the Boonta Eve race, and his reputation would have been made.»
  - Ch14 L1019 «How might Anakin have fared under Qui-Gon's guidance? he wondered.»
  - Ch14 L1019 «So eager to live up to the memory of Qui-Gon that he was continually overlooking Anakin's attempts to live up to him.»
  - Ch14 L1053 «Qui-Gon reprimanding Jar Jar Binks for his uncouth behavior.»
  - Ch16 L1141 «"Master Qui-Gon would have been proud of you."»
  - Ch19 L1525 «Was that what Qui-Gon had intuited so many years earlier on Tatooine, and had motivated him to attack with such resolve the Sith who had revealed himself in that parched landscape?»
  - Ch22 L1691 «A Trade Federation blockade of Naboo, rumors of a Chosen One found on a desert world, the death of Qui-Gon Jinn at the hands of a Sith...»
  - Ch22 L1713 «All at once, here was Qui-Gon Jinn's former Padawan, right under Dooku's nose.»
  - Ch30 L3061 «A product of Sienar Advanced Projects Laboratory, the craft had been modified - - perhaps unwittingly by Sienar itself - - for the Sith who had killed Qui-Gon Jinn.»
  - Ch33 L3653 «That was Qui-Gon.»
  - Ch39 L4229 «And sometimes when the current was especially forceful, when it surged as if descending from great heights, he could hear the voice of Qui-Gon Jinn, as clearly as if he were still alive.»
  - Ch39 L4245 «Someone birthed by the Force itself, Qui-Gon would have said - - never a doubt that Anakin's mother might have been lying.»
  - Ch48 L4831 «As Qui-Gon had told him, he needed to make his focus his reality.»
  - Ch48 L4833 «Qui-Gon, who had died - - even though, to his young mind, Jedi weren't supposed to...»

### 5.9 Minor and lore figures: Maul, Ventress, Sifo-Dyas, Jango, Plagueis/Bane, Durge/Aurra Sing (individual profiles)

#### 5.9.1 Darth Maul (lore, Sidious's former apprentice)
- **What LoE says:** «the savage Darth Maul, who, in fact, had been nothing more than a minion, like Asajj Ventress and General Grievous» (L1703). «Sidious had once confessed that he had erred similarly in his training of Darth Maul.» (L3361). «Ventress and Maul had been driven by a desire to excel - - to be the best - - instead of merely allowing themselves to be pure instruments of the dark» side (L3361). He trained in The Works (L2437), and the Duelist Elite droids cut apart there point to «Sidious's previous apprentice» (L3685).
- **Scaling implications:** a Sith-internal evaluation. Dooku and Sidious both treat Maul as a flawed instrument.
- **Complete quote register (6 lines: every sentence that names Maul, verbatim, in book order):**
  - Ch8 L425 «Whether a replacement for the fearsome Darth Maul, or a Sith even during his years in the Jedi Order, Gunray didn't care to know.»
  - Ch22 L1703 «Perhaps he had been working with other potential replacements for his earlier apprentice, the savage Darth Maul, who, in fact, had been nothing more than a minion, like Asajj Ventress and General Grievous.»
  - Ch26 L2437 «Strong in the dark side - - made so by Darth Sidious - - the building had been the place of Dooku's apprenticeship, just as it had served as a training ground for Darth Maul before Dooku, and who knew who or how many other Sith disciples before Maul.»
  - Ch32 L3329 «Two was bad enough - - in the form they had been used by Darth Maul, or in Anakin Skywalker's sad attempt to employ the technique on Geonosis.»
  - Ch32 L3361 «Sidious had once confessed that he had erred similarly in his training of Darth Maul.»
  - Ch32 L3361 «Ventress and Maul had been driven by a desire to excel - - to be the best - - instead of merely allowing themselves to be pure instruments of the dark side.»

#### 5.9.2 Asajj Ventress (lore)
- **What LoE says:** Anakin's face is «scarred from a contest with Dooku-trained Asajj Ventress» (L39). Grievous lumps her with «mere distractions» (L1079: «Dooku's misguided apprentice, Asajj Ventress»). Dooku's self-critique: «He had made the same mistake with Grievous that he had made with Ventress, by allowing her to fill herself with hate, as if hate could substitute for» the Force (L3361); «When he should have been helping Ventress rid herself of self, he had instead permitted her to grow impassioned.» (L3361). She also uses the two-blade style Dooku deplores (L3327).
- **Scaling implications:** none new. Dooku rates Ventress and Grievous as the same kind of failure.
- **Complete quote register (8 lines: every sentence that names Ventress, verbatim, in book order):**
  - Ch1 L39 «His face scarred from a contest with Dooku-trained Asajj Ventress.»
  - Ch11 L675 «Dooku was known to have trained Jedi in the dark arts - - Jedi Knights who had lost faith in the ideals of the Republic, Padawans fascinated by the power of the dark side, misinformed novices such as Asajj Ventress, who had been mentored by a Jedi - - but the question remained, who, if anyone, had been Dooku's teacher?»
  - Ch15 L1079 «Perhaps Gunray had thought of him as some mindless entity, like the reawakened Gen'Dai, Durge; or Dooku's misguided apprentice, Asajj Ventress; or the human bounty hunter called Aurra Sing - - all three of whom had been so driven by personal hatred of the Jedi that they had proved worthless, mere distractions while Grievous went about the real business of war.»
  - Ch22 L1703 «Perhaps he had been working with other potential replacements for his earlier apprentice, the savage Darth Maul, who, in fact, had been nothing more than a minion, like Asajj Ventress and General Grievous.»
  - Ch32 L3327 «It had merely bothered him that Ventress and lesser combatants such as the bounty hunter Aurra Sing had adopted the foul practice.»
  - Ch32 L3361 «He had made the same mistake with Grievous that he had made with Ventress, by allowing her to fill herself with hate, as if hate could substitute for dispassion.»
  - Ch32 L3361 «When he should have been helping Ventress rid herself of self, he had instead permitted her to grow impassioned.»
  - Ch32 L3361 «Ventress and Maul had been driven by a desire to excel - - to be the best - - instead of merely allowing themselves to be pure instruments of the dark side.»

#### 5.9.3 Sifo-Dyas (lore)
- **What LoE says:** Yoda: «Knew of Dooku's imminent departure, Master Sifo-Dyas did.» / «But worried about Master Dooku, Sifo-Dyas was.» (L713); «But killed Sifo-Dyas was, before on Kamino Jango Fett arrived.» (L753). Dooku's view: «Dooku's true confidant had been Master Sifo-Dyas, who, while also disturbed by what was occurring, was too weak to take action.» (L1693). Sidious's order: «But Master Sifo-Dyas cannot stand, for the Jedi cannot learn about the army until we are prepared to have them learn of it.» (L1703).
- **Scaling implications:** none. His murder is Dooku's initiation as Tyranus.
- **Complete quote register (17 lines: every sentence that names Sifo-Dyas, verbatim, in book order):**
  - Ch11 L711 «Most of all, with Master Sifo-Dyas."»
  - Ch11 L713 «But worried about Master Dooku, Sifo-Dyas was.»
  - Ch11 L713 «Saw in Dooku the effect of Qui-Gon's death, Sifo-Dyas did.»
  - Ch11 L713 «"Knew of Dooku's imminent departure, Master Sifo-Dyas did.»
  - Ch11 L723 «"Could Sifo-Dyas have foreseen that, as well?"»
  - Ch11 L731 «"Master, did Sifo-Dyas order the clone army? "»
  - Ch11 L745 «That Sifo-Dyas the order placed; that Tyranus the donor clone furnished.»
  - Ch11 L745 «Seen by the Kaminoans, neither Sifo-Dyas nor Tyranus was.»
  - Ch11 L747 «"From Sifo-Dyas?"»
  - Ch11 L751 «"Could Tyranus have been an alias for Sifo-Dyas?»
  - Ch11 L753 «But killed Sifo-Dyas was, before on Kamino Jango Fett arrived."»
  - Ch11 L761 «"Could Sifo-Dyas and Tyranus have been partners?"»
  - Ch11 L765 «Could he have killed Sifo-Dyas?"»
  - Ch11 L803 «But why hadn't Yoda told him sooner about Sifo-Dyas?»
  - Ch22 L1693 «Dooku's true confidant had been Master Sifo-Dyas, who, while also disturbed by what was occurring, was too weak to take action.»
  - Ch22 L1703 «But Master Sifo-Dyas cannot stand, for the Jedi cannot learn about the army until we are prepared to have them learn of it.»
  - Ch22 L1705 «And so with the murder of Sifo-Dyas, Dooku had embraced the dark side fully, and Sidious had conferred on him the title Darth Tyranus.»

#### 5.9.4 Jango Fett (lore)
- **What LoE says:** the clone template, who trained the early ARCs (L85); hired by Gunray to kill Padmé (L235); Yoda: «But playing both sides Jango Fett clearly was.» (L763).
- **Scaling implications:** none.
- **Complete quote register (5 lines: every sentence that names Jango Fett, verbatim, in book order):**
  - Ch1 L85 «Early-generation ARCs had received training by the Mandalorian clone template, Jango Fett.»
  - Ch2 L135 «All these years later, he could still recall his brief conversation with Jango Fett, on Kamino.»
  - Ch4 L235 «It was personal because of Gunray's hiring of Jango Fett to assassinate Padme three years ago - - first with a bomb planted on her ship, then with the pair of kouhuns a changling had inserted into Padme's Senatorial quarters on Coruscant.»
  - Ch11 L753 «But killed Sifo-Dyas was, before on Kamino Jango Fett arrived."»
  - Ch11 L763 «But playing both sides Jango Fett clearly was.»

#### 5.9.5 Darth Plagueis and Darth Bane (lore)
- **What LoE says:** Dooku knows little of Sidious's past, «of his Master, Darth Plagueis, even less» (L2451). The Rule of Two is traced to Bane (L1677).
- **Scaling implications:** none. Nothing changes in the Plagueis entries.
- **Complete quote register (2 lines: every sentence that names Sith lore, verbatim, in book order):**
  - Ch22 L1673 «Rarely more than two in each generation, from Darth Bane forward, Master and apprentice would devote themselves to harnessing the strength that flowed from the dark side, and to making the most of every opportunity to allow darkness to wax.»
  - Ch26 L2451 «Of his youth, Sidious had offered little these past thirteen years; of his Master, Darth Plagueis, even less.»

#### 5.9.6 Durge and Aurra Sing (lore)
- Grievous lists Durge with Ventress as a «mere distraction» (L1079); Aurra Sing is one of the «lesser combatants» who use two blades (L3327). No feats.
- **Complete quote register (3 lines: every sentence that names Durge and Aurra Sing, verbatim, in book order):**
  - Ch15 L1079 «Perhaps Gunray had thought of him as some mindless entity, like the reawakened Gen'Dai, Durge; or Dooku's misguided apprentice, Asajj Ventress; or the human bounty hunter called Aurra Sing - - all three of whom had been so driven by personal hatred of the Jedi that they had proved worthless, mere distractions while Grievous went about the real business of war.»
  - Ch29 L2959 «For a moment he was back on Muunilinst, jousting with Durge's speeder-freak lancer droids.»
  - Ch32 L3327 «It had merely bothered him that Ventress and lesser combatants such as the bounty hunter Aurra Sing had adopted the foul practice.»

### 5.10 Other named characters with real roles (individual profiles)

Every named side character with a real role in LoE gets a profile here, followed by the complete register of sentences that name them. Named characters with no real role (one or two passing mentions, so no profile): Dodonna, Zan Arbor, Seti Ashgad, Lapiz, Aayla Secura, Ylenic It'kla, K'Kruhk, Daakman Barrek, Tarr Seir, Cin Drallig, Sun Fac.

#### 5.10.1 Padmé Amidala (*Padmé, 19 BBY, secretly pregnant*)
- **Personality:** loyal to Palpatine longer than her allies and frank with him: «"You underestimate Palpatine's acuity," Padme said. "Besides, he appreciates frank speech."» (L631).
- **Arc:** in the loyalist delegation stalled by Pestage (Ch10) → meets Palpatine (Ch13) → shares a near-telepathic bond with Anakin (Ch14) → is evacuated, hurt in the skimmer crash with Bail and Mon Mothma (Ch42, 46) → watches the abduction news in Nicandra Plaza and reaches for Anakin: «Anakin, she said to herself, as if she could reach him with a thought.» (L5133).
- **Relationships:** Anakin (secret husband; he senses she has changed, L827); Palpatine (her old patron, whose teasing makes her blush); Bail and Mon Mothma (allies who are drifting toward opposition).
- **Scaling implications:** none for combat. Her Force-like link to Anakin is a narrative device, not a feat.
- **Complete quote register (80 lines: every sentence that names Padme, verbatim, in book order):**
  - Ch1 L101 «But I would have thought Senator Amidala of greater interest to you than Supreme Chancellor Palpatine."»
  - Ch4 L235 «It was personal because of Gunray's hiring of Jango Fett to assassinate Padme three years ago - - first with a bomb planted on her ship, then with the pair of kouhuns a changling had inserted into Padme's Senatorial quarters on Coruscant.»
  - Ch8 L423 «Playing a game of his own, teasing Dooku along, he had informed Dooku that a precondition to their entering into any exclusive arrangement was the death of former Naboo Queen Padme Amidala, who had foiled Gunray on two occasions, and had been the loudest opposition voice at his trials.»
  - Ch8 L423 «Dooku had hired a bounty hunter to oversee the business, but two attempts at assassinating Senator Amidala had failed.»
  - Ch8 L423 «But just when Gunray finally had Amidala in his grasp - - on trial, no less, for espionage - - Dooku had equivocated, refusing to have Amidala killed outright, and not lifting a hand against the Jedi until some two hundred of them had showed up with a clone army the Republic had grown in secret!»
  - Ch10 L619 «Padme Amidala, of Naboo; Mon Mothma of Chandrila; human Senators Terr Taneel, Bana Breemu, and Fang Zar; and alien Senator Chi Eekway.»
  - Ch10 L623 «Padme hugged him, as well, though somewhat stiffly.»
  - Ch10 L627 «Padme scolded him with a frown.»
  - Ch10 L631 «"You underestimate Palpatine's acuity," Padme said.»
  - Ch10 L635 «Padme glanced at everyone.»
  - Ch10 L649 «"You most certainly were informed," Padme said, "since the appointment was secured through your office."»
  - Ch11 L763 «With the changeling who targeted Amidala, an intermediary he was."»
  - Ch11 L781 «"If Gunray hadn't been so keen on assassinating Padme Amidala,"»
  - Ch12 L819 «Probably because of Anakin's own musings about what might have become of him had the Jedi not been forced to land on Tatooine to find a replacement part for Padme's starship.»
  - Ch12 L823 «He might never have met Padme...»
  - Ch12 L823 «Padme didn't know then that his precocity wasn't limited to a skill for building and fixing things.»
  - Ch12 L827 «But Padme remained his Angel - - A vision of her broke his reverie.»
  - Ch12 L871 «Anakin suppressed an impulse to reveal his marriage to Padme, as well.»
  - Ch12 L891 «"Senator Amidala, for example.»
  - Ch13 L909 «"Senator Organa, Senator Amidala - - all of you.»
  - Ch13 L927 «"They have, Supreme Chancellor," Padme said.»
  - Ch13 L963 «"Then we've made a beginning," Padme said.»
  - Ch13 L965 «"Senator Amidala, is that not the droid Jedi Skywalker constructed?"»
  - Ch13 L967 «Padme looked at C-3PO.»
  - Ch13 L973 «He glanced at Padme.»
  - Ch13 L975 «Padme said in surprise.»
  - Ch13 L977 «"Why Senator Amidala, I do believe you're blushing."»

#### 5.10.2 Bail Organa (*Bail, 19 BBY*)
- **Personality:** principled and wary of the militarized Coruscant: «The omnipresence of the soldiers seemed to bother Bail more than it did his few friends on Coruscant or his peers in the Senate.» (L587). «Bail vowed to speak openly of his concerns, and he began to cultivate friendships with Senators who shared those concerns.» (L617).
- **Arc:** the POV for Ch10 → in the delegation → pilots the skimmer that is downed by Vultures (Ch42) → in the crash aftermath gives the book's clearest political analysis: «Palpatine will quietly accrue more and more power. Without realizing it, the Separatists have played right into his hands by launching this attack.» (L4717).
- **Relationships:** friends with Obi-Wan, Yoda, Mace and Tiin (L599); Mon Mothma; Padmé.
- **Scaling implications:** none.
- **Complete quote register (85 lines: every sentence that names Bail Organa, verbatim, in book order):**
  - Ch10 L579 «Bail Organa had just arrived on Coruscant, and was still wearing the deep blue cloak, ruffle-collared shirt, and knee-high black boots his wife had laid out for him for the trip from Alderaan.»
  - Ch10 L579 «Just thinking about his beautiful blue-and-white homeworld made Bail yearn to be there, yearn for the company of his loving wife.»
  - Ch10 L581 «Bail motioned to the identichip he had already slotted in the scanner.»
  - Ch10 L583 «The helmeted noncom glanced at the display screen, then looked down at Bail.»
  - Ch10 L585 «Bail sighed in exasperation and fished into the breast pocket of his brocaded tunic for his credit chip.»
  - Ch10 L587 «The omnipresence of the soldiers seemed to bother Bail more than it did his few friends on Coruscant or his peers in the Senate.»
  - Ch10 L595 «"You're free to go," the trooper said at last, apparently satisfied that Bail was in fact who his credentials claimed him to be.»
  - Ch10 L595 «Bail laughed to himself.»
  - Ch10 L595 «Bail hailed a free-travel air taxi and instructed the droid driver to take him to the Senate Building.»
  - Ch10 L595 «Even outside the normal skylanes, above the myriad and abysmal canyons that fissured the urbanscape, far from patrols of security soldiers or the prying eyes of Republic spies, Coruscant looked much as it had for as long as Bail had known it.»
  - Ch10 L599 «To the extent that friendships could be forged with them, Bail had come to know a few: Jedi Masters Obi-Wan Kenobi, Yoda, Mace Windu, Saesee Tiin - - the privileged few who also were allowed to meet personally with Palpatine.»
  - Ch10 L601 «Bail stirred in his seat.»
  - Ch10 L601 «According to Bail Antilles, at any rate, Bail's predecessor in the Senate.»
  - Ch10 L601 «Thirteen years ago the Senate was interested only in ridding itself of Finis Valorum, Antilles had once told Bail.»
  - Ch10 L603 «Still, Bail couldn't help but wonder who might have succeeded Palpatine as Supreme Chancellor if the Separatist crises on Raxus Prime and Antar 4 had not occurred when they did, just as Palpatine's term of office was ending.»
  - Ch10 L613 «Bail could recall clearly the day he had stood with Palpatine, Mas Amedda, Malastarian Senators, and others on a balcony of the Senate Office Building, watching tens of thousands of clone troopers march into the enormous ships that would take the war to the Separatists.»
  - Ch10 L615 «Regardless, Bail had put his feelings aside and had played his part, endorsing bills he might have previously denounced, supporting Palpatine's "efficient streamlining of cumbersome bureaucracy."»
  - Ch10 L617 «And with him, tractable Bail Organa.»
  - Ch10 L617 «Bail vowed to speak openly of his concerns, and he began to cultivate friendships with Senators who shared those concerns.»
  - Ch10 L619 «Slender, short-haired Mon Mothma hurried to embrace Bail as he approached.»
  - Ch10 L621 «"A momentous occasion, Bail," she said into his left ear.»
  - Ch10 L623 «Bail laughed to himself.»
  - Ch10 L623 «A bit more full-faced than Bail remembered, but the very picture of classic beauty in her elegant robes and elaborate coiffure.»
  - Ch10 L625 «"An extraordinary world, Naboo," Bail said.»
  - Ch10 L627 «"He's not stubborn, Bail.»
  - Ch10 L645 «Bail came to his feet, speaking for everyone when he said: "It shouldn't be.»
  - Ch10 L655 «Bail spoke up.»
  - Ch10 L659 «Pestage looked at Bail.»
  - Ch10 L661 «Anger mottled Bail's bearded face.»
  - Ch13 L903 «In the reception area of the holding office, Bail Organa paced restlessly.»
  - Ch13 L907 «"We're nothing if not patient," Bail said.»
  - Ch13 L909 «Just then Palpatine himself appeared, glancing at Bail and the others, then at Pestage.»

#### 5.10.3 Mon Mothma (*19 BBY*)
- **Role:** the Chandrilan Senator of the delegation («Slender, short-haired Mon Mothma hurried to embrace Bail as he approached.», L619). She raises the constitutional amendments (L955), survives the skimmer crash (Ch46: «"Xi Char monstrosities," Mon Mothma said.», L4709) and is armed in the aftermath (L4743).
- **Scaling implications:** none.
- **Complete quote register (20 lines: every sentence that names Mon Mothma, verbatim, in book order):**
  - Ch10 L619 «Padme Amidala, of Naboo; Mon Mothma of Chandrila; human Senators Terr Taneel, Bana Breemu, and Fang Zar; and alien Senator Chi Eekway.»
  - Ch10 L619 «Slender, short-haired Mon Mothma hurried to embrace Bail as he approached.»
  - Ch13 L955 «"And these amendments to the Constitution," Mon Mothma started to say.»

  - Ch42 L4433 «The Jedi led Padme, C-3PO, Bail, and Mon Mothma to the open-roofed skimmer.»
  - Ch46 L4681 «Into focus swam the faintly smiling face of Mon Mothma.»
  - Ch46 L4683 «"No sleeping on the job, Senator," Mon Mothma said, as if from underwater.»
  - Ch46 L4685 «Her head was pillowed on Mon Mothma's left arm, and her ears felt as if they were plugged with cotton.»
  - Ch46 L4689 «"Just for a moment," Mon Mothma said in the same watery tone.»
  - Ch46 L4693 «Mon Mothma regarded her in knowing silence, then extended a hand to help her climb from the craft.»
  - Ch46 L4697 «Mon Mothma hurried her away from the skimmer, to where Bail and C-3PO were hiding behind the blockish pedestal of a modernistic sculpture.»

  - Ch53 L5129 «Three among a crowd several hundred strong standing in the Nicandra Plaza, Padme, Bail, and Mon Mothma watched the late-breaking news report on the Embassy Mall's HoloNet monitor.»

#### 5.10.4 The loyalist delegation: Fang Zar, Terr Taneel, Bana Breemu, Chi Eekway
- **Role:** the Senators who lobby Palpatine with Padmé, Bail and Mon Mothma (L619). Fang Zar is the spokesman: «"It can be hoped," Fang Zar said, "not counted on."» (L641); «"Why not consider ceding some of those worlds," Fang Zar suggested.» (L935).
- **Scaling implications:** none. These Senators seed the Delegation of 2,000 in ROTS.
- **Complete quote register (10 lines: every sentence that names Loyalist delegation, verbatim, in book order):**
  - Ch10 L619 «Padme Amidala, of Naboo; Mon Mothma of Chandrila; human Senators Terr Taneel, Bana Breemu, and Fang Zar; and alien Senator Chi Eekway.»
  - Ch10 L629 «"For all the good it will do," Chi Eekway said, displeasure wrinkling her blue face.»
  - Ch10 L633 «"We've been nothing if not frank, Senator," dark-complected, bib-bearded Fang Zar said.»
  - Ch10 L639 «Eekway nodded gravely.»
  - Ch10 L641 «"It can be hoped," Fang Zar said, "not counted on."»
  - Ch10 L651 «"Several of us have risked much and traveled great distances," Eekway added.»
  - Ch10 L657 «"We're not asking for much of his time," Terr Taneel said, in a more placating tone.»
  - Ch13 L933 «Eekway said.»
  - Ch13 L935 «"Why not consider ceding some of those worlds," Fang Zar suggested.»
  - Ch13 L959 «"If we can be assured of a certain... exhalation of power," Bana Breemu said.»

#### 5.10.5 Captain Jules Dyne, Republic Intelligence (*19 BBY*)
- **Personality:** a methodical forensic analyst, dry under pressure: «"Abandoned, perhaps, but not forgotten," Captain Dyne said from behind Valiant.» (L3073).
- **Arc:** authenticates the Sidious hologram and the IBC code (Ch14, 17) → is attached to Mace's raid on The Works (Ch30) → tracks the slipper and boot prints to 500 Republica (Ch34–38) → continues the search without the Jedi (L4331) → finds Sidious's lair and dies there.
- **Death:** two Geonosian sonic weapons kill the whole team: «He saw the commandos drop in their tracks, as if blown over by a gale-force wind.» (L4537); «It was as if every bone in his body had been made pliant.» (L4541). As corrected in Appendix A, this was a sonic attack, not a Force feat. His last sight is a holotable showing «a Trade Federation battleship, halved and in flames» (L4541).
- **Scaling implications:** none for combat. He is the only Republic character who physically finds Sidious's lair.
- **Complete quote register (86 lines: every sentence that names Dyne, verbatim, in book order):**
  - Ch14 L1023 «The dark-haired, robust-looking analyst Captain Dyne perched himself on the edge of a shipping container.»
  - Ch14 L1027 «"The chair bears the stamps of several of the manufacturers affiliated with Dooku," Dyne continued.»
  - Ch14 L1035 «"We think so," Dyne said.»
  - Ch14 L1039 «Dyne glanced from Anakin to Obi-Wan.»
  - Ch14 L1045 «Dyne explained.»
  - Ch17 L1311 «"Ten years ago it would have had all the makings of a full-blown diplomatic incident," Intelligence officer Dyne was explaining to Yoda and Mace Windu in the data room of the Jedi Temple.»
  - Ch17 L1315 «"Since they've been forced to make do with subcontracting work," Dyne said.»
  - Ch17 L1319 «Dyne shook his head.»
  - Ch17 L1325 «"But come to tell us of these things, Captain Dyne hasn't."»
  - Ch17 L1327 «Dyne grinned.»
  - Ch17 L1331 «Dyne paused, then added: "Remember the missing credits that became the basis for accusations leveled against Chancellor Valorum back in the day?"»
  - Ch17 L1337 «Dyne opened an alloy briefcase and removed a ribbed data cell.»
  - Ch17 L1343 «Dyne paused the message before Grievous had faded from view.»
  - Ch17 L1347 «"Deep in the Outer Rim," Dyne said.»
  - Ch17 L1351 «Again his gaze fell on Dyne.»
  - Ch17 L1353 «Dyne shook his head.»
  - Ch17 L1357 «Dyne looked from Mace to Yoda and back again.»
