  - Ch50 L4943 «Fencing with his four adversaries - - for that's all the fight amounted to - - Grievous saw six of the soldiers and three of the Red Guards jolted to spasming deaths by the MagnaGuards' double-tipped scepters.»
  - Ch50 L4957 «Elsewhere his three surviving elites were doing well against five Red Guards.»
  - Ch51 L4995 «Unless, of course, he had received prior intelligence on the number of Red Guards in Palpatine's detail, and the number of troopers and other combatants stationed in the bunker complex.»
  - Ch51 L4999 «The same was true for those Red Guards whose corpses had been burned open by lightsaber.»

#### 5.10.21 Finis Valorum (lore)
- **What LoE says:** «As ineffectual a leader as he was, Finis Valorum had tried to comport himself as if he placed the Force above all.» (L3177). The «suspicious» explosion of his freighter is among Palpatine's suspected crimes (L615), and Valorum-era credits turn up in the Sidious money trail (Ch17, L3527).
- **Scaling implications:** none.
- **Complete quote register (10 lines: every sentence that names Valorum, verbatim, in book order):**
  - Ch8 L421 «In much the same way that the Trade Federation had amassed an army right under the nose of Supreme Chancellor Finis Valorum, Dooku - - in plain sight - - was seeing to it that Baktoid Armor Workshops was supplying weapons to any corporations that agreed to ally with him.»
  - Ch10 L601 «Thirteen years ago the Senate was interested only in ridding itself of Finis Valorum, Antilles had once told Bail.»
  - Ch10 L603 «Valorum, who had believed he could put honesty on the Senate agenda.»
  - Ch10 L615 «The sudden disappearance of Senator Seti Ashgad after he had argued against installation of surveillance cams in the Senate Building; the suspicious explosion of a star freighter aboard which Finis Valorum was a passenger; the passage of a security bill that granted Palpatine wide-ranging powers over Coruscant...»
  - Ch17 L1331 «Dyne paused, then added: "Remember the missing credits that became the basis for accusations leveled against Chancellor Valorum back in the day?"»
  - Ch17 L1335 «"The credits that allegedly disappeared into the pockets of Valorum's family members on Eriadu were routed through Aargau."»
  - Ch22 L1689 «His discontent had continued to grow and fester; his frustration with the Republic Senate, with ineffectual Supreme Chancellor Valorum, with the shortsightedness of the Jedi Council members themselves.»
  - Ch27 L2561 «Having suffered through all four hours of Palpatine's State of the Republic address to the Senate, interrupted dozens of times by standing ovations - - an archaic tradition not practiced since the era of Supreme Chancellor Valorum Eixes - - Bail Organa watched from the backseat of the air taxi as a trio of assault cruisers lifted off into Coruscant's flame-orange sky, casting their wedge-shaped shadows on the spired roof of the Jedi Temple.»
  - Ch31 L3177 «As ineffectual a leader as he was, Finis Valorum had tried to comport himself as if he placed the Force above all.»
  - Ch33 L3527 «Intelligence discovered that the building belonged to a corporation called LiMerge Power, which was believed to have been involved in the manufacture and distribution of prohibited weapons during Finis Valorum's term as Supreme Chancellor.»

## 6. Scaling connections (cross-book; I read nothing from the other workers' files)
- **→ *Revenge of the Sith* (film/novel):** LoE ends where ROTS begins. Its setups feed four ROTS fights: (a) **Dooku vs Anakin+Obi-Wan**, staged as a test with Sidious controlling the outcome (L5261); (b) **Obi-Wan vs Grievous** on Utapau, foreshadowed by «I fear that you will end up beyond the repair of even the Geonosians» (L3351) and by Sidious's own mention of Utapau (L3483); (c) **Mace vs Sidious**, where Mace's sword level vs Grievous (Ch47) is one input; (d) **Yoda vs Sidious**, with Dooku's «one eye closed» foresight comparison (L4193). *I did not open books/revenge-of-the-sith-novel.txt or research/_extract/rots\*. The ROTS worker should check the chest-crush continuity and the Invisible Hand duel against L5261.*
- **→ *Star Wars: Clone Wars* (2003 micro-series):** Hypori (L1083, L3343, L5005), the Dantooine seismic tank (L4793), Mon Calamari (L4793). There is a **continuity conflict** over Grievous's raid on Coruscant (see §5.1).
- **→ *Yoda: Dark Rendezvous* (Vjun):** L2451 («Carry a darkness within me, I do»), L3335, L4665 (the Temple door open), L4835 (Anakin's grenade on Vjun).
- **→ *Shatterpoint*:** Vaapad named as Mace's edge (L4813).
- **→ *Darth Plagueis*:** the Plagueis name drop (L2451) only. *I did not open books/darth-plagueis.txt.*
- **→ Existing research files:** mace-comicvine-respect.txt (L43, L58) quotes the Ch47 lines; I checked them against the book and both match. grievous-legends-quotes.txt L103 says Wookieepedia claims Grievous "could duel Mace Windu evenly even with limited movement" (wiki text, not a book quote). **The novel does not support "evenly"**: Mace is «on and all over» him, wins the exchange and ends it at will (L4811–4821). debates-yoda-mace-sidious.md t3667 quotes L2451 (matches, apart from the dash).

## 7. Verdict implications (diff leans)

Scale (verdicts.md convention): **low diff** ≈ the winner takes it 8.5/10 or more; **mid** ≈ 6.5–8; **high** ≈ 5.5–6.

| Matchup (versions) | LoE evidence | Lean |
|---|---|---|
| **Grievous (19 BBY, pre-crush) vs a typical Jedi Knight, or several** | Ch50 (4 Knights at once, «mere novices» L4939, «Fencing» L4943); Ch54 (2 better Knights, one move); Hypori «Power moves» (L3343) | **Grievous, low diff.** Up to about four Knights at once is still low diff |
| **Grievous vs Kit Fisto / Shaak Ti / Stass Allie class (mid Council)** | He ranks Kit with Mace (L4943). Kit shreds the MagnaGuards (L4815). Dooku: «I pity you should you have to face off against any of the Council Masters» (L3343). Shaak Ti survived Hypori (L5005) | **The Council Master wins, mid diff** (Kit). Shaak Ti and Stass are closer: high diff or even. Grievous's adaptive computers favor him in long fights |
| **Mace (19 BBY, Vaapad) vs Grievous (19 BBY)** | ★ Duel 1; Grievous's own concession (L4929) | **Mace, low–mid diff (≈ 8/10).** Not low, because Grievous adapted «close enough» (L4817) to Vaapad and a half-twist nearly beheaded Mace. Not high, because Mace led every phase and was never touched |
| **Dooku (19 BBY) vs Grievous (19 BBY)** | «hard-pressed» (L1669) but «kept some secrets» (L1671); the spar-demo disarm (L3349); «Hopeless» (L3361) | **Dooku, mid diff (≈ 7.5/10)**: Grievous's speed and strength make Dooku work; Dooku has the Force (lightning, TK) and finesse in reserve |
| **Obi-Wan (19 BBY) vs Grievous (19 BBY)** | Dooku's ladder puts Kenobi with Drallig among those whose «responses» Grievous should fear (L3347) | **Obi-Wan, mid diff** (consistent with ROTS; ROTS itself is not judged here) |
| **Yoda vs Grievous** | «stars help you, Yoda» (L3347) | **Yoda, low diff** |
| **Dooku (19 BBY) vs Anakin (19 BBY), 1v1** | Sidious expects Dooku to win unless by «fluke» (L5261, A-grade). Dooku: Anakin is «a powerful opponent» who has «grown only more powerful» (L2521) and is «potentially a greater threat to the Sith than anyone realized» (L4663). The rage-amped ceiling collapse (L4381). Obi-Wan: «On Vjun, you needed a grenade» (L4835) | **Calm Anakin: Dooku, high diff (≈ 6/10).** **Rage-amped Anakin: Anakin, mid–high diff (≈ 6.5/10).** LoE adds no duel; this lean comes from statements, with ROTS's outcome taken as given |
| **Dooku (19 BBY) vs Anakin + Obi-Wan (19 BBY), 2v1** | They pierced his Quey'tek (L4215); «how powerful they had become in partnership» (L4663); Sidious orders him to kill Obi-Wan first (L5261) | **Duo, high diff, slight lean (≈ 5.5–6/10).** Dooku's best line is separating them, as he does in ROTS |
| **Sidious (19 BBY) vs Mace (19 BBY)** | No fight. Sidious hid from Mace face-to-face (Ch23), clouded the search (L3773–3777) and had Mace chase a decoy (Ch50). Mace's best sword result here is vs Grievous, far below Sidious-tier | **No change to the existing Sidious ≈ 7/10.** LoE adds information-warfare dominance (Sidious picks the time and place), which supports Sidious |
| **Sidious (19 BBY) vs Yoda (19 BBY)** | «Such mastery was one of the skills that distinguished Sidious from Yoda» (L4193, Dooku knew both); «powerful enough to hide in plain sight» (L4245, Yoda's own view); Yoda feels him only when he acts (L4531). Contrast: Sidious and Yoda «neither was entirely what he appeared to be» (L2451) | **No change (Sidious ≈ 6/10).** LoE supports Sidious in foresight and concealment, the prep side |
| **Sidious vs Dooku** | Crash course (L2445); Dooku can't read the lip twitch for sure (L3377); Dooku is a disposable test piece (L5261) | **Sidious, low diff (unchanged, high confidence)** |
| **Yoda vs Dooku** | Dooku's admission of Yoda's «easy parrying and, indeed, handling of the Sith lightning» (L2451); Yoda learned the new Sith's danger only by fighting Dooku (L4243) | **Yoda, mid diff (unchanged 8/10).** Adds a Dooku-POV admission against interest. His «fled … to safeguard the plans» line is C-grade |
| **Mace vs Dooku** | Dooku's ladder «Mace Windu or, stars help you, Yoda» (L3347) puts Mace above Kenobi/Drallig and below Yoda; Dooku «warned» Grievous about Mace's blade (L4929) | **Slight strengthening of Mace ≈ 6/10.** Dooku's own respect for Mace's blade is new primary evidence |
| **Anything Plagueis** | One name mention (L2451) | **No implication.** |
| **Grievous vs Maul (TPM) / vs Sion / tier placement** | Nothing direct. Dooku > Grievous (mid) and Dooku > Maul (6.5/10); Mace > Grievous (≈8/10) | **Provisional: Grievous below Dooku and Maul**, so bottom of tier 6 or top of tier 7. Grievous vs Maul is not judged; my lean is Maul, mid–high diff, on the "finesse/unpredictability" weakness (L3333), but that is reasoning, not evidence |

## 8. Biggest scaling takeaways (summary)
1. **Peak Grievous is a Knight-killer, low diff:** six Jedi Knights die on-page with no meaningful damage to him; bolts deflected by hand; saber glancing blows only «add a new odor» (L4951).
2. **Grievous's ceiling is the Council tier:** Mace beats him in a short exchange (terrain + push). Dooku says he'd lose to «any of the Council Masters» (L3343) and ranks the threats: Drallig/Kenobi < Windu < «stars help you, Yoda» (L3347).
3. **Mace's line on Grievous** («as forceful as any Mace had ever had to counter … astonishing» speed, L4813) is the best Legends hype for Grievous, and the adaptive-computer mechanic (L4815–4817) means he gets more dangerous the longer a fight goes.
4. **Dooku > Grievous, but not free:** «hard-pressed» / «kept some secrets» (L1669–1671). verdicts.md L177 should reflect that nuance.
5. **Going into ROTS, the Sith Lords themselves rate Anakin as close to Dooku:** «grown only more powerful» (L2521), «greater threat to the Sith than anyone realized» (L4663), and Sidious's «fluke» clause with a promise to «control the outcome» (L5261). The Invisible Hand duel is a staged test.
6. **Rage-amped Anakin** brings down a 30 m archive hall with a howl (L4381). Dooku has to leap clear and can't rule it out as real (L4661).
7. **Sidious's power is foresight and concealment:** «streams of possibilities … could be surprised … but not for long» (L4193); «powerful enough to hide in plain sight» (L4245); Yoda feels him only once he acts (L4531); and the explicit Sidious > Yoda foresight comparison (L4193).
8. **Yoda > Dooku gets another admission against interest** (Yoda's «easy parrying», L2451). **Plagueis is named only once** (L2451), so nothing changes there.

## 9. Quote verification report
- **Script:** `research/work-labyrinth/verify_loe_quotes.py` (adapted from `research/verify_kotor_quotes.py`).
  - **What it checks:** it extracts every «…» span and normalizes both the span and the book: curly and straight single quotes and backticks become ', curly double quotes become ", whitespace is collapsed, and dash runs become the source's " - - ".
  - **Fragments:** it splits the span on `...` and on ` / ` and requires every fragment of 3+ characters to be an exact substring of the book.
  - **Second check:** the book line holding the first fragment must sit within ±3 of an `L####` cited on the same notes line.
- **Hand-curated quotes (§1–§8 and the final section):** **602 checked, 602 verified, 0 failed.**
  - 0 line-proximity warnings remain. The one warning during drafting was on «secrets», a common word that appears at five lines; I fixed it by citing L1671 on that notes line.
  - **Failures found and fixed during drafting (7):**
    - «few, if any … even Dooku» was joined across a " / " break. I added " / " as a fragment separator.
    - “he no longer slept” was a paraphrase, replaced with the exact «General Grievous did not sleep.» (L1423).
    - “he could respect the Jedi for that” was a case error, fixed to «He could…».
    - “quote” was a format placeholder.
    - “power moves” was a case error, fixed to «Power moves».
    - Two Wookieepedia phrases had been put in guillemets. They are now in plain quotes and marked as wiki text, not book text.
  - Line-cite fixes: the L4381 collapse line and the L5261 «powerful ally» line.
  - A paraphrase was corrected without a quote failure: Shaak Ti «found it difficult to get a sense» of Palpatine (L4575). My first draft said she "can't read" him.
- **Appendix B concordance:** **2,622 entries, 2,622 verified, 0 failed.** These are machine-extracted, so the check confirms the extraction and normalization.
- **Appendix A raw log:** 252 double-quoted fragments were checked separately; 252 match after 7 fixes:
  - Six were punctuation or wording slips: «Half a hundred», «Been there, done that,», «Stop,» … «I concede.», «Because you cannot be married to both.», «Dooku!» … «I will kill you!» and «crowning achievement,».
  - One was a quote-mark slip in the "misstep" line.
- **Re-run after the 2026-10-19 gap fix (America/New_York): 4,527 guillemet quotes, 4,527 verified, 0 failures, 0 line-proximity warnings** for the whole file. New material:
  - §5.8–5.10 now hold 34 individual profiles (previously two grouped entries), each followed by a complete auto-harvested register of every sentence that names the character. That adds 1,107 register lines, plus the profile prose quotes.
  - Appendix B.0 adds 94 hand-curated pronoun-only and epithet-only lines: Grievous 25, Anakin 23, Sidious 15, Dooku 10, Obi-Wan 6, Thal K'sar 4, Mace 3, Yoda 2, Shaak Ti 2, TC-16 2, Padmé 1, Dyne 1.
  - 5 quote failures and 2 line warnings were caught in the new prose and fixed before insertion (three bracketed completions, one truncated quote, two mis-cited lines).
- *(Pre-fix figure, kept for the record:)* **Grand total in the file: 3,224 guillemet quotes, 3,224 verified, 0 failures.**
- **OCR caveat:** quotes keep the scan's spelling; see the header for the list of *(sic)* items. Verifying against an OCR text proves fidelity to this file, not to the printed page. Where an OCR slip is obvious (e.g. «l ife», «Palpatinc»), the meaning is unaffected.

## Appendix A. Raw chapter reading log (working notes written while reading, kept as-is apart from the quote fixes listed in §9)
The quotes in this log use plain double quotes ("…"), not guillemets, so they are outside the main verification count. A separate check of the log's quoted fragments is reported in §9. The `#### ChN` headers below are log entries, not document sections.

#### Ch1 (L19-107) Cato Neimoidia orchard. Obi-Wan (Council member) & Anakin (Knight, braid cut after Praesitlyn, scar from Ventress, mech hand). Obi-Wan deflects bolts back into 2 droids; Anakin decapitates droid near OW's head ("your head was where my lightsaber needed to go"). Anakin Force push flattens 2 droids (L59). Commander Cody, Dodonna holo. Plan: go in with harvester beetles. OW teases about Padme/Palpatine.
#### Ch2 (L109-151) Run under beetles; mines; Anakin leaps through minefield guided by Force (L127), severs beetle pincers, rescues commando; Anakin Force-heals clone's shock (L143). Droidekas roll down.
#### Ch3 (L153-231) Anakin calls EMP + SPHA-T strike; "Hero with no Fear" (L161). Inside nest: OW banks deflected bolt off 4 surfaces into door panel (L201) -"The beauty of Form Three" (L207). Anakin: "You've always been better at evasion". Split: OW bait, Anakin to capture Gunray.
#### Ch4 (L233-261) Anakin POV: personal grudge vs Gunray; secret marriage; anger "a kind of fuel" (L241); "Twice he might have been able to kill Dooku himself had Obi-Wan not held him back" (L241). Pockets OW's rebreather (Force-calls it L243). SBDs; TC-16 blows cover.
#### Ch5 (L263-295) OW POV on Qui-Gon, Dooku "consummate planner, consummate duelist" (L265). "Clearly Anakin was as strong in the Force as any Jedi who had ever sat on the Council." (L267). Spores (dislocating) leak; OW without rebreather.

#### Ch7 (L345-385) Spore-drunk OW holds vs "better than fifty droids" (L351), slow-mo zone (L349). Anakin: "If only Mace, Kit, or Shaak Ti could have seen you." Gunray escaped. Commandos found something.
#### Ch8 (L387-473) Gunray/Haako on core ship. Backstory: Sidious at Dorvalla/Eriadu/Naboo; Sidious quietly kept trials from verdicts (L417). Dooku revealed he is Sith, Master = Sidious (L425). Gunray: "The Republic has yet to see the face of its real enemy." Mechno-chair left behind (hyperwave transceiver given 14 yrs ago). "Grievous! What if Grievous should find out?" (L457). Grievous: "Once merely a barbaric living being; now a cyborg monstrosity... Already the butcher of entire populations; the devastator of countless worlds..." (L459).
#### Ch9 (L475-573) Mechno-chair walks off w/ R2; OW senses danger and pulls Anakin down before poison gas (L565); score "Thirty-six" saves. Anakin yanks cable, shocked; hologram of Sidious + Gunray "my Lord Sidious".

KEY: "few, if any, Jedi would be capable of defeating him. ## Appendix B. Script-built concordance: every sentence that names each major character

Built by `research/work-labyrinth/concordance.py` from the source file. **Rule:** a paragraph of 320 characters or less that names the character is quoted whole (this catches short dialogue with its speaker tag). In a longer paragraph, every sentence containing an alias is quoted. Aliases are listed per character. **Limits:** sentences that refer to a character only by pronoun inside long paragraphs are *not* captured here; the hand-curated §3–§4 add those (all duel exchanges, for example). A sentence naming two characters appears under both. The version tag for each list is given in its header; the backstory exceptions are noted.


### B.0 Hand-curated pronoun-only and epithet-only lines (94 quotes)

The concordance below (B.1+) is alias-based, so any sentence that refers to a character only as "he", "she", "the Jedi", "the Sith Lord", "his Master", "the Bith", "the protocol droid" and so on is missing from it. This subsection adds those lines by hand. Every line is significant, does NOT name the character (or names them only by an epithet outside the alias list), was assigned to that character by reading the context, and is not already quoted in this file. Format: chapter, source line, `[PRONOUN/EPITHET]`, version tag (per the header version key), verbatim quote. Added 2026-10-19 and verified by `verify_loe_quotes.py`.

**Grievous** (25)

- Ch15 L1079 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Grievous, Clone Wars backstory (22–20 BBY)] «Sometimes he wondered just how many clones he had killed or wounded that day.»
- Ch15 L1083 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Grievous, Clone Wars backstory (22–20 BBY)] «He admired the precision and care that had gone into the construction of the lightsabers; more, each seemed to retain a faint memory of its wielder.»
- Ch15 L1083 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Grievous, Clone Wars backstory (22–20 BBY)] «The revelation of his existence had had to wait until a handful of hapless Jedi had arrived on the foundry world of Hypori.»

- Ch15 L1105 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Grievous, 19 BBY (LoE, pre-crush)] «His two feet, which also resembled claws, were capable of increasing his height by several centimeters.»
- Ch15 L1105 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Grievous, 19 BBY (LoE, pre-crush)] «Sheathed in metal more suited to a starfighter, his skeletal upper limbs were spread wide, clawlike duranium hands just touching the hatchway frame.»
- Ch18 L1379 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Grievous, Kaleesh (organic, pre-cyborg)] «To be denied a warrior's death - - as was his due!»

- Ch8 L415 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Sidious, 19 BBY (LoE)] «There at Dorvalla and Eriadu, manipulating events to shunt power and influence to the Neimoidians; there at Naboo, ordering a blockade of the planet, the murder of Jedi, assassination of the Queen... a debacle for the Trade Federation.»
- Ch12 L875 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Sidious, 19 BBY (LoE)] «You know my love for the Republic knows no bounds.»
- Ch23 L1807 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Sidious, 19 BBY (LoE)] «Am I to infer from this that you no longer trust me?"»
- Ch25 L2341 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Sidious, 19 BBY (LoE)] «The designs he sent were like nothing I had ever seen.»
- Ch28 L2873 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Sidious, 19 BBY (LoE)] «But the guy I handed the yoke over to - - he was no Jedi.»
- Ch31 L3179 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Sidious, 19 BBY (LoE)] «Despite what you may think - - and notwithstanding the extraordinary powers the Senate has deemed fit to bestow on me these past five years - - I am one voice in a welter.»
- Ch31 L3255 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Sidious, 19 BBY (LoE)] «"As I've told you on many occasions, I do not have the luxury of seeing this world through the Force.»
- Ch39 L4193 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Sidious, 19 BBY (LoE)] «His mastery of the dark side of the Force endowed him with the power to decipher the currents that comprised the future, and to comprehend that while those currents were manifold, they were not boundless.»
- Ch39 L4243 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Sidious, 19 BBY (LoE)] «For that reason he understood just how dangerous this new Sith Lord was.»
- Ch40 L4325 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Sidious, 19 BBY (LoE)] «Several hours into the cursory search, Dyne had been struck by the possibility that certain Coruscanti - - perhaps the Sith Lord himself - - could be helping to coordinate the attack.»
- Ch43 L4545 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Sidious, 19 BBY (LoE)] «The fact that the man had made no attempt to mask himself assured and comforted Dyne that he was about to die, and shortly after the realization, he did.»
- Ch44 L4567 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Sidious, 19 BBY (LoE)] «"Master Ti, while I appreciate your concern, I've no need of rescue.»
- Ch46 L4717 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Sidious, 19 BBY (LoE)] «"Not only will he live it down, he'll profit from it.»

- Ch49 L4885 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Sidious, 19 BBY (LoE)] «His hair and robes were mussed, and he looked pale and feeble among his striking protectors.»

**Anakin** (23)

- Ch6 L307 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (rage-amped)] «He called instead on his anger, bringing images to mind to fuel his rage.»
- Ch6 L309 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (Knight)] «He brought his will to bear on the task, but the lightsaber could accomplish only so much, even in the hands of a powerful Jedi.»
- Ch12 L823 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (Knight)] «He had an uncanny sense for knowing what was going to happen; a certainty that he would become celebrated.»
- Ch12 L825 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (Knight)] «He was different - - chosen long before the Jedi Order had bestowed the title.»
- Ch12 L825 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (Knight)] «Mythical beings came to him - - Angels and Jedi - - and he excelled in contests in which humans weren't even meant to participate.»
- Ch12 L831 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (Knight)] «He waved his hand, calling on the Force to keep the mechnochair fixed in place.»
- Ch12 L869 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (Knight)] «It happened with your mother; it will happen again.»
- Ch12 L869 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (Knight)] «You can dream, you have imagination and vision.»
- Ch19 L1469 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (Knight)] «Wending through clouds of expanding fire, he locked down the trigger of the laser cannons and made a second pass through the enemy wave, destroying a dozen more fighters in a heartbeat.»
- Ch19 L1513 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (Knight)] «But a Jedi unafraid to call on his rage.»
- Ch19 L1513 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (Knight)] «Oh, to have the lightsaber of that one dangling from his belt, he thought.»
- Ch19 L1525 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (Knight)] «Was he immune to real harm, or - - as someone born to restore balance to the Force - - did he require defenders to guide him to that destiny?»
- Ch25 L2287 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (Knight)] «Propelled by the Force, as well as the force of the explosion, he struck the arched ceiling and fell hard to the floor.»
- Ch25 L2307 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (Knight)] «Evading blaster bolts with perfectly timed jumps and rolls, he got close enough to the droids to wave some of them into others, toppling them as if they had been picked up by a strong wind.»
- Ch25 L2315 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (Knight)] «And either you answer my questions, or I jettison you here, and let the Gossams do what they will with you."»
- Ch29 L2995 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (Knight)] «Then, leaping from the now rocketing sled, he called on the Force to propel himself toward the swaying cage.»
- Ch39 L4191 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (Knight)] «In their unabashed zeal to capture him they had actually piloted their starfighters straight through the roof of the largest of the facility's containment domes, and had managed to survive.»
- Ch45 L4663 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (rage-amped)] «Assuming that he had grown powerful enough to have collapsed the dome, the end result was simply further evidence that he would someday undo himself.»
- Ch48 L4829 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (Knight)] «He was as afraid to lose her as he was to hold on to her, and the pain of that contradiction made him wish he had never been born.»
- Ch55 L5261 [PRONOUN/EPITHET] [Anakin, 19 BBY (Knight)] «Perhaps he never will be prepared.»

### B.1 Grievous: 280 entries. Aliases: `Grievous|the general|the cyborg|cyborg general`. Version: Grievous, 19 BBY pre-crush (unless the line is Kaleesh/Geonosis/Hypori backstory, Ch15/18/22)

- Ch8 L457: «"Are you brain-dead?" Gunray fairly screeched. "Grievous! What if Grievous should find out?"»
- Ch8 L459: «Supreme Commander of the droid armies, General Grievous had been San Hill and Poggle the Lesser's gift to Dooku. Once merely a barbaric living being; now a cyborg monstrosity, devoted to death and destruction. Already the butcher of entire populations; the devastator of countless worlds...»
- Ch15 L1059: «From the bridge of the Trade Federation cruiser the Invisible Hand - - flagship of the Confederacy fleet - - General Grievous observed the whole mad dance.»
- Ch15 L1059: «To any other spectator it might appear that the Viceroy was risking his wattled neck, but Grievous knew better.»
- Ch15 L1059: «Late to arrive at the rendezvous because of his decision to detour to Cato Neimoidia, Gunray was putting on a show for the general's benefit, attempting to make it seem that he had been chased to the Outer Rim when, in fact, he had undoubtedly allowed his hyperspace vectors to be plotted by Republic forces.»
- Ch15 L1061: «At another time Grievous might have applauded their bravery by allowing them to escape with their lives, but Gunray's transparent attempts at pretense had exposed the fleet to surveillance, and now the Republic pilots would have to die.»
- Ch15 L1063: «Grievous turned from the cruiser's forward viewports to the weapons stations, where a pair of rangy droids were monitoring the pursuit.»
- Ch15 L1067: «Grievous swung back to the viewport in time to see the half-dozen hyperdrive rings come apart in short-lived explosions.»
- Ch15 L1067: «A consequence of the Neimoidians' reluctance to augment the droid brains of the fighters with interface capabilities, Grievous noted.»
- Ch15 L1073: «Grievous wondered for a moment if any of the pilots might be Jedi, in which case he would opt to capture rather than kill.»
- Ch15 L1077: «Grievous pictured Gunray, Haako, and the others strapped into luxurious acceleration couches, shivering with dread, perhaps sorry for the brief detour to Cato Neimoidia, wondering how a handful of Republic pilots had so easily decimated their squadrons, certainly comlinking the core ship to dispatch reinforcements.»
- Ch15 L1079: «From their first acquaintance, Gunray had made the mistake of treating Grievous as just another droid - - even though he had been told that this was not the case.»
- Ch15 L1079: «Perhaps Gunray had thought of him as some mindless entity, like the reawakened Gen'Dai, Durge; or Dooku's misguided apprentice, Asajj Ventress; or the human bounty hunter called Aurra Sing - - all three of whom had been so driven by personal hatred of the Jedi that they had proved worthless, mere distractions while Grievous went about the real business of war.»
- Ch15 L1079: «The attitude of the Neimoidians had changed quickly enough, though, in part because they had been witness to Grievous's capabilities, but more as a result of what had occurred on Geonosis.»
- Ch15 L1079: «Had it not been for Grievous, Gunray and the rest might have suffered the same fate as Poggle the Lesser's lieutenant, Sun Fac.»
- Ch15 L1079: «Grievous's actions in the catacombs that day - - with the Geonosians retreating by the thousands from the arena and companies of clone commandos following them in - - had allowed Gunray to escape the planet alive.»
- Ch15 L1081: «Grievous regretted that he hadn't been able to see the reactions, but he, too, had been forced to flee as Geonosis fell.»
- Ch15 L1083: «By then, Grievous had already amassed a sizable collection of lightsabers, but at Hypori he had been able to add several more, two of which he wore inside his command cloak even now.»
- Ch15 L1085: «Grievous had become infamous during the long conflict: conquering worlds, defeating grand armies, exterminating entire colonies of Huk.»
- Ch15 L1087: «In what passed for negotiations - - fifty Jedi Knights and Masters ready to loose their lightsabers on Grievous and his army - - the Kaleesh were made to appear the aggressors.»
- Ch15 L1087: «Sanctions and reparations were imposed; traders avoided the planet; Grievous's people starved and perished by the hundreds of thousands.»
- Ch15 L1089: «Ultimately the InterGalactic Banking Clan had come to their rescue, helping with funds, reinstating trade, providing Grievous with a new direction. Years later, the Muuns would come again...»
- Ch15 L1091: «Grievous's eyes tracked the course of the now imperiled shuttle.»
- Ch15 L1093: «"Launch tri-fighters to assist the shuttle," Grievous instructed the gunners.»
- Ch15 L1095: «Calling for magnification of the shuttle pursuit, Grievous saw that the stragglers were newly minted ARC-170s, copiloted crafts equipped with powerful laser cannons at the tips of their outstretched wings and multiple torpedo launchers.»
- Ch15 L1097: «Grievous sharpened his gaze.»
- Ch15 L1097: «What impressed Grievous was how quickly the pilots came to each other's assistance.»
- Ch15 L1099: «Without glancing at the bridge gunners, Grievous said: "Finish them off.»
- Ch15 L1103: «Grievous!»
- Ch15 L1103: «Gunray would have liked nothing more than to inform Dooku of the general's actions, but he feared that the Sith would stand with Grievous.»
- Ch15 L1105: «Abruptly everyone fell silent, and a moment later General Grievous appeared in the hatchway, the rounded crown of his elongated death mask of a helmet grazing the top of the opening, his high-backed collar of ceramic armorplast reminiscent of a neck brace.»
- Ch15 L1105: «His campaign cloak, slit down one side from left shoulder to floor, was thrown back so that twin pectorals of armor plating were exposed, along with the reverse ribs that began at Grievous's hip girdle and extended upward to his shielded sternum.»
- Ch15 L1109: «Gunray felt the gazes of everyone in the cabin fall on him. His distrust of the cyborg was no secret; nor was Grievous's enmity for him. "And I can only assume that you were very troubled by the prospect, General."»
- Ch15 L1115: «"I am your keeper, Viceroy. Your protector." Striding into the cabin, he began to circle the table, stopping directly behind Gunray, towering over him. Peripherally, Gunray saw Haako slouch deeper into his chair, refusing to look either at him or at Grievous, circling his hands in a nervous gesture.»
- Ch15 L1117: «"I have no favorite among you," the general said at last.»
- Ch15 L1121: «"If none offers, Chairman, then I will take one." Grievous walked to the hatchway, his talons screeching along the deck. "For now, return to your separate vessels. When a world has been selected, I will contact each of you in the usual manner, and provide you with new rendezvous coordinates."»
- Ch17 L1339: «"General Grievous," Yoda said, narrowing his eyes.»
- Ch17 L1341: «"You'll be pleased to learn that I've chosen a world for us, Viceroy," Grievous was saying. "Belderone will be our temporary home." The cyborg fell silent for a moment. "Viceroy? Viceroy!" Whirling to someone off cam, he barked: "End transmission."»
- Ch17 L1343: «Dyne paused the message before Grievous had faded from view. "As high-resolution an image as I've ever seen," he said. "Technology of a different order than we're used to seeing - - even from the Confederacy."»
- Ch17 L1355: «Mace paced away from the table. "Belderone is not a highly populated world, but it is friendly to the Republic. Grievous will kill millions just to make a point." He glanced at Yoda. "We can't let that happen."»
- Ch17 L1357: «Dyne looked from Mace to Yoda and back again. "If Republic forces are waiting when Grievous attacks, the Separatists will realize that we've managed to eavesdrop on their transmissions."»
- Ch17 L1371: «"Wait, the lead will. Needed Obi-Wan and Anakin will be." Yoda turned to the still image of General Grievous. "Prepare carefully for this battle, we must."»
- Ch18 L1375: «In dreams, Grievous remembered his life. His mortal life.»
- Ch18 L1385: «They would support the planet financially, assume its staggering debt, if Grievous would agree to serve the clan as an enforcer.»
- Ch18 L1385: «Both to save his world and to provide himself with a touch of the life he had known as a warrior, a strategist, a leader of armies, Grievous had accepted the offer.»
- Ch18 L1387: «Still, Grievous wasn't entirely proud of his decision.»
- Ch18 L1387: «And Grievous's previous notoriety was such that it could not be eclipsed.»
- Ch18 L1389: «That was when San Hill had paid him a second visit. Something consequential in mind. Obvious even to someone who could barely see straight. "We can keep you alive," rail-thin Hill had whispered into Grievous's unimpaired ear.»
- Ch18 L1393: «"I have my mind," Grievous had said. "What I lack is a body."»
- Ch18 L1399: «Two words had caught Grievous's attention: battle droids.»
- Ch18 L1403: «His interest piqued, Grievous had said: "A war begun by whom? The Banking Clan? The Trade Federation?"»
- Ch18 L1423: «. In dreams he remembered his past life. But in fact, he was not dreaming, for dreams were a product of sleep, and General Grievous did not sleep.»
- Ch18 L1425: «But Grievous was aware of a problem even before the displays told him as much.»
- Ch18 L1435: «"Roll the ship starboard to minimize our profile, and reorient the deflector shields. Deploy all wings of droid tri-fighters and ready all port-side batteries for enfilade fire." Grievous braced himself against a bulkhead as the cruiser was shaken by an explosion.»
- Ch18 L1439: «Grievous quickened his pace.»
- Ch18 L1439: «Grievous took a moment to study the deployment of the Republic ships and starfighter squadrons.»
- Ch18 L1441: «Grievous was stunned.»
- Ch19 L1461: «Accustomed to Anakin's disregard for call signs, Obi-Wan answered in kind. "Anakin, there are approximately five hundred droids positioned between Grievous and us. What's more, the capital ships are too heavily shielded."»
- Ch19 L1501: «"We've got a clear shot at Grievous!" he told Obi-Wan. With its tapered bow and large outrigger fins, the general's cruiser resembled a classic-era Coruscant skyscraper laid on its side.»
- Ch19 L1511: «Anakin pictured Grievous standing stalwart behind the transparisteel viewports. "A taste of what's coming when we meet in the flesh," he growled.»
- Ch19 L1513: «Grievous's reptilian eyes tracked the audacious maneuvers of the yellow-and-green starfighter that was attempting to strafe the bridge.»
- Ch19 L1513: «Grievous could see that in the pilot's dauntless determination, his abandon.»
- Ch19 L1515: «Grievous was confident that, if pressed, he could turn the tide of battle, but that was not his present mandate.»
- Ch19 L1523: «Obi-Wan wondered if he had ever witnessed a more lucid demonstration of the Jedi axiom than Anakin's Force-centered, death-defying harassment of Grievous's ship.»
- Ch19 L1527: «Obi-Wan thought for a moment that Grievous was actually going to bring all forward guns to bear on Anakin.»
- Ch19 L1529: «Of course Grievous realized that his barbaric act had caught the Republic forces by surprise, but he had nothing more in mind than escape into hyperspace.»
- Ch19 L1537: «"Vessels of the main fleet are safely away," a droid reported to Grievous as soon as the cruiser entered hyperspace. "Expected arrival at the alternate rally point: ten standard hours."»
- Ch19 L1539: «"Losses at Belderone?" Grievous said.»
- Ch19 L1543: «Grievous ran the fingers of his clawlike hand down the bulkhead.»
- Ch20 L1547: «"Trained well by Dooku, General Grievous was," Yoda said.»
- Ch20 L1555: «The muscles in Mace's jaw bunched. "Billions have died in this war. Belderone was saved, and, more importantly, we were able to keep Grievous on the run."»
- Ch21 L1571: «"General Grievous has left the docking bay," a Trade Federation lieutenant relayed to Gunray in his lavish quarters in the core ship's port-side command tower.»
- Ch21 L1579: «The Neimoidian guards stationed there had also been alerted to Grievous's arrival.»
- Ch21 L1587: «"Immediately on being apprised by Shu Mai of the Belderone rendezvous, I contacted Grievous, expressing anger that he hadn't informed me personally. I accused him of purposely leaving me out of the command loop."»
- Ch21 L1599: «Gunray saw that Grievous was accompanied by four of his elite MagnaGuards.»
- Ch21 L1599: «Fearsome bipedal battle droids built to exacting specifications, they stood as tall as the general and were armed with combat staffs tipped with electromagnetic pulse generators.»
- Ch21 L1599: «Benefiting from Grievous's own programming, as well as from the instruction Grievous had received from Dooku, the elite were trained in the Jedi arts, and more than a match for most.»
- Ch21 L1601: «Grievous's elite didn't even slow down.»
- Ch21 L1603: «Grievous glared into the lens of the holocam mounted outside the hatch. "Admit us, Viceroy. Or shall I instruct my elite to lay waste to everything that stands between me and you?"»
- Ch21 L1613: «"Viceroy!" Grievous rasped.»
- Ch21 L1627: «Grievous glowered at him. "They will when they learn what you've done."»
- Ch21 L1637: «Grievous raised his right claw and pointed to Gunray.»
- Ch21 L1651: «Grabbing Gunray by the neck, Grievous lifted him a meter off the floor.»
- Ch22 L1657: «Poor Gunray, Dooku thought. Pitiful creature... But for having left the mechno-chair behind on Cato Neimoidia, he deserved all the fear Grievous had put into him.»
- Ch22 L1659: «Secluded in his castle on Kaon, Dooku had just spoken with the general and was pondering how best to handle the situation.»
- Ch22 L1659: «While the incident at Belderone wasn't conclusive proof that the Republic had managed to decrypt the Separatist code and intercept Grievous's transmission to Gunray, it was prudent to assume that this was the case.»
- Ch22 L1659: «Dooku had already ordered the general to refrain from using the code for the time being.»
- Ch22 L1659: «Clues to secrets that would astonish even Grievous.»
- Ch22 L1661: «After the Sith Lord had expressed interest in Grievous to Dooku, Dooku, in turn, had expressed interest in Grievous to Chairman San Hill, of the InterGalactic Banking Clan.»
- Ch22 L1663: «Poor Grievous, Dooku thought.»
- Ch22 L1663: «During the Huk War, and later, while in the employ of the IBC, Grievous had survived numerous attempts on his life, so an assassination attempt was ruled out almost immediately.»
- Ch22 L1663: «What if Grievous should actually die in the crash?»
- Ch22 L1665: «But Grievous had survived - - and only too well.»
- Ch22 L1667: «Grievous certainly believed that he had always been the cold-blooded conqueror he was now, when in truth his cruelty and prowess owed much to his rebuilding.»
- Ch22 L1669: «Grievous had been a delight to train, as well.»
- Ch22 L1669: «The Geonosians had arranged for Grievous to be nothing but anger and rage.»
- Ch22 L1669: «And as to the general's combat skills, few, if any, Jedi would be capable of defeating him.»
- Ch22 L1669: «There had been moments during the extensive combat sessions when even Dooku had been hard-pressed to outduel the cyborg.»
- Ch22 L1673: «Manipulation of the sort that had gone into the transformation of Grievous went to the heart of what it meant to be a Sith - - if, indeed, the words heart and Sith could be used together.»
- Ch22 L1703: «Perhaps he had been working with other potential replacements for his earlier apprentice, the savage Darth Maul, who, in fact, had been nothing more than a minion, like Asajj Ventress and General Grievous.»
- Ch23 L1743: «"A Jedi operation," Palpatine said slowly. "And by all accounts you, that is, the Jedi, were successful in routing General Grievous's forces."»
- Ch23 L1745: «"A rout it was not," Yoda said. "To hyperspace Grievous fled. But protecting the Separatist leaders, he was."»
- Ch23 L1791: «"May I inquire as to how the Jedi learned of Grievous's plan to attack Belderone?"»
- Ch23 L1793: «"A hyperwave transceiver that belonged to Viceroy Gunray was seized at Cato Neimoidia," Mace explained. "The device allowed Intelligence to decipher the Separatist code. A message transmitted by General Grievous to Viceroy Gunray regarding Belderone was monitored, and we acted on it."»
- Ch26 L2523: «"My lord, the Jedi may search for others who contributed to fashioning the communications devices you distributed to Gunray, myself, and others. Also, there is the matter of Grievous's defeat at Belderone."»
- Ch31 L3263: «"To end this war, more we will have to do than defeat Grievous and his army of war machines. More we will have to do than seize remote worlds."»
- Ch31 L3301: «"And General Grievous?"»
- Ch31 L3305: «"More machine than alive, Grievous is - - though more dangerous for it. But without Dooku's or Sidious's leadership, collapse the Separatists will. Bound together by the Sith they are. Mortared by the dark side of the Force."»
- Ch32 L3327: «In a forward hold of Grievous's flagship, Dooku watched the cyborg general duel with his elite MagnaGuards, three of his trophy lightsabers in constant motion, parrying thrusts of the guards' pulse-weaponed staffs, slicing the recycled air a hairbreadth from the expressionless faces of his opponents, incapacitating arm and leg servos when he could.»
- Ch32 L3327: «Grievous was a force to be reckoned with, to be sure, but Dooku deplored his habit of collecting lightsabers.»
- Ch32 L3327: «Grievous's habit struck Dooku as the worst kind of profanation.»
- Ch32 L3329: «The only aspect of Grievous's technique that vexed him more was the general's penchant for using four blades.»
- Ch32 L3331: «Grievous was fast, and so were his IG 100-series sparring partners.»
- Ch32 L3333: «Dooku had taught Grievous well, and Grievous had taught his elite well.»
- Ch32 L3333: «But they were not invincible, not even Grievous, because they could be confused by unpredictability, and they had no understanding of finesse.»
- Ch32 L3339: «Grievous and his guards were dancing. Going through their programmed motions. An Ataro attack answered by Shii-Cho; Soresu answered by Lus-ma... Dooku couldn't suffer another moment of it.»
- Ch32 L3341: «"No, no, stop, stop," he yelled, coming to his feet and striding to the middle of the training circle, his arms extended to both sides. When he was certain that he had their attention, he swung to Grievous.»
- Ch32 L3349: «He flicked his blade quickly, ridding two of the guards of their staffs, then placing the glowing tip a millimeter from Grievous's death-helmeted visage.»
- Ch32 L3353: «His vertically slit eyes unfathomable, Grievous nodded.»
- Ch32 L3361: «He had made the same mistake with Grievous that he had made with Ventress, by allowing her to fill herself with hate, as if hate could substitute for dispassion.»
- Ch32 L3365: «He watched Grievous disarm two of the MagnaGuards.»
- Ch32 L3365: «In fact, Grievous was all instrument.»
- Ch32 L3365: «My time is at hand, he told himself as he centered himself proudly on the transmission grid, Grievous behind him, down on one knee, with head lowered.»
- Ch32 L3413: «"What is thy bidding, Master?" Grievous asked from behind Dooku.»
- Ch32 L3415: «Sidious turned slightly toward Grievous, but continued to speak to Dooku.»
- Ch32 L3419: «"My fleet stands ready, Master," Grievous said, still without raising his gaze from the grid.»
- Ch32 L3421: «"The Republic is monitoring you?" Sidious asked the general.»
- Ch32 L3431: «Again Dooku was stunned. So, too, was Grievous.»
- Ch32 L3433: «"Is that wise, Master," the general asked carefully, "after what happened at Belderone?"»
- Ch32 L3459: «Dooku saw Grievous's armorplast head elevate in surprise.»
- Ch32 L3477: «Grievous made a meaningful sound.»
- Ch33 L3551: «"Grievous is on Tythe?"»
- Ch35 L3863: «"Yes," Palpatine was saying. "Dooku is the key to everything just now. Despite all our victories in the inner systems... Do you suspect he and General Grievous may have some secret strategy?"»
- Ch37 L3951: «"Just once I wish we could repay Dooku and Grievous in kind," Anakin said over the tactical net, as Red Squadron dropped from the belly of the Integrity and rocketed toward Tythe.»
- Ch37 L3979: «"No signature for Grievous's cruiser," he said to Obi-Wan. "None of the ships of the Separatist leadership are here."»
- Ch39 L4293: «"It's Grievous. But he can't be planning to occupy Coruscant. There aren't enough battle droids in the entire galaxy for that."»
- Ch39 L4299: «"Not Grievous - - Sidious."»
- Ch39 L4305: «"Demoralize Coruscant, Grievous will. Harry those who live in the heights and who wield power. Send them fleeing for safer havens, the attack will. Disrupt the Senate."»
- Ch42 L4393: «Grievous's strike was impeccably timed.»
- Ch42 L4397: «Even if the rumors proved true, Padme was convinced that Palpatine would find some way to drive Grievous from the Core - - again.»
- Ch42 L4397: «And there was always the likelihood that Grievous would single out civilian targets, as he had done on countless occasions.»
- Ch43 L4449: «Flagship of the Separatist flotilla, General Grievous's kilometers-long cruiser the Invisible Hand held to a stationary orbit above Coruscant's Senate District, just now in full sunlight, the most majestic of its forest of aeries standing tall above the clouds.»
- Ch43 L4449: «Grievous studied the images for a moment before returning to his customary place at the forward viewscreens.»
- Ch43 L4449: «In the first moments of the sneak attack, Grievous had caught a few of the ships with their shields contracted, and those hapless few glided now like flaming torches above Coruscant's pearl-strung night side, fire-suppression tenders and rescue ships following in their wake, gobbling up escape pods and lifeboats.»
- Ch43 L4451: «From the point of view of Republic naval commanders, it must have appeared that Grievous lacked a plan; that desperation resulting from his previous defeats in the Mid and Outer Rims had driven him to gather what remained of his fleet and hurl it into a battle he couldn't possibly hope to win.»
- Ch43 L4453: «And indeed, Grievous was doing everything he could to encourage that misconception.»
- Ch43 L4455: «Smoke billowing from some of the capital world's deepest canyons told Grievous that a few of the spearhead droids had succeeded in evading both shields and antiaircraft fire.»
- Ch43 L4455: «Similarly, tentative maneuvers on the part of Coruscant's home fleet vessels told him how eager their commanders were to break formation and engage Grievous head-on.»
- Ch43 L4457: «Anticipating as much, Grievous had planted surprises for those Republic battle groups closest to the Core, surprises in the form of mass-shadow mines, and had stationed warships at reversion points along the hyperlanes.»
- Ch43 L4459: «Grievous took a long moment to absorb the silent battle that flared beyond the thick transparisteel of the bridge viewports. He loathed being so far from the action and bloodshed. But he knew that he had to be patient a while longer. Then all the waiting and frustration would be justified.»
- Ch43 L4463: «"This is not unexpected," Grievous said, without turning from the view. "Instruct Group One commanders that they should continue targeting orbital mirrors and communication satellites. Relocate the jamming platform to zero-one-zero ecliptic, and intensify the shields."»
- Ch43 L4467: «Grievous glanced at the tactical table.»
- Ch43 L4467: «"Override the survival and engagement programs of those droid fighters," Grievous ordered.»
- Ch43 L4483: «Sooner than expected, Grievous thought.»
- Ch43 L4483: «It was warcraft of a sort Grievous had never practiced.»
- Ch43 L4493: «"Yes, sir." Grievous turned from the viewports. "Is the strike force assembled?"»
- Ch43 L4501: «Grievous nodded. "That should suffice."»
- Ch43 L4507: «Jamming transmissions from this sector of Coruscant, Grievous is."»
- Ch43 L4509: «The Jedi - - a brown-haired human female named Lari Oll - - lifted her hands from the console and shook her head in confusion. "How could Grievous - - "»
- Ch43 L4519: «"Hmph. On Grievous's objective, that depends. Leave Coruscant soon and only slightly bruised, he might. Wait, we must, until he reveals his plan." Yoda paused to consider his own words, then leaned his weight on the gimer stick and looked hard at Lari Oll. "Enabled the comm is?"»
- Ch43 L4529: «Grievous was loosing his war machines against both military and civilian targets, firing at anything and everything that wandered into his sights, commanding his droid fighters to dash themselves against Coruscant's defensive umbrellas or race down through traffic lanes, initiating chain reactions of collisions.»
- Ch44 L4609: «Grievous!»
- Ch44 L4615: «By then, there was little to see but angry exchanges of cyan and crimson light. She turned to face Isard. "Alert Homeworld Security that General Grievous has broken through the perimeter."»
- Ch44 L4623: «"Negative," Shaak Ti said, shaking her head. "Grievous knew enough to come here. We have to assume that the escape route has been compromised, as well."»
- Ch44 L4635: «Allie waited until the Red Guards had gathered around him. "Now - - quickly!" When everyone had moved into the hallway, Shaak Ti used her comlink to find Mace Windu. "Mace, Grievous is onworld," she said the moment she heard his voice.»
- Ch44 L4649: «"Grievous's forces must have destroyed the orbital mirror that feeds this sector.»
- Ch45 L4663: «And he wished that General Grievous could have been there to witness the display for himself.»
- Ch45 L4665: «What if Grievous was outwitted and destroyed at Coruscant?»
- Ch45 L4671: «"A recorded message," the droid announced. "From General Grievous."»
- Ch45 L4673: «"Play it!" Dooku said as he hurried up the sloop's aft boarding ramp and into the instrument-filled main hold. A paused holoimage of the cyborg floated in blue light. Throwing off his dusty cape, Dooku paced while the FA-4 triggered the recording to replay.»
- Ch45 L4675: «"Lord Tyranus," Grievous said, in motion suddenly and genuflecting. "Supreme Chancellor Palpatine will soon be ours."»
- Ch46 L4721: «Bail continued. "Dooku, Grievous, Gunray, Palpatine."»
- Ch47 L4769: «How had Grievous known to attack 500 Republica?»
- Ch47 L4771: «How had Grievous known?»
- Ch47 L4771: «More important, how had Grievous known that Palpatine wasn't to be found in either of his offices?»
- Ch47 L4771: «It was conceivable that Dooku had furnished Grievous with data on hyperlanes that skimmed the outer limits of the Deep Core.»
- Ch47 L4771: «Similarly, Dooku could have supplied Grievous with the orbital coordinates of specific communications satellites and mirrors, or with tactical information regarding the location of dedicated shield generators on the surface.»
- Ch47 L4773: «But Palpatine had only just been elected Supreme Chancellor when Dooku left Coruscant to return to Serenno, and back then, some thirteen years ago, Palpatine had been living in a high-rise tower close to the Senate Building. So how had Grievous known to go to 500 Republica?»
- Ch47 L4777: «So had Dyne and the commandos fallen victim to Grievous's attack?»
- Ch47 L4807: «"Grievous," Mace grumbled. Kit glanced at him. "Here we go again."»
- Ch47 L4809: «Three cars distant marched General Grievous and two of his elite droids, their capes snapping behind them in the wind, pulse-tipped batons angled across their barrel chests.»
- Ch47 L4811: «Without pausing, Grievous drew two lightsabers from inside his billowing cloak.»
- Ch47 L4811: «By the time they were ignited, Mace was already on and all over the cyborg, batting away at the two blades, swinging low at Grievous's artificial legs, thrusting at his skeletal face.»
- Ch47 L4811: «In a corner of Mace's mind he wondered to which Jedi Grievous's blades had belonged.»
- Ch47 L4811: «Just as the Force was keeping Mace from being blown from the mag-lev's roof, magnetism of some sort was keeping the general fastened in place.»
- Ch47 L4811: «For the cyborg, though, the coherence hindered as much as it helped, whereas Mace never remained in one place for very long.»
- Ch47 L4813: «As Mace already knew from Ki-Adi-Mundi and Shaak Ti, Grievous was well trained in the Jedi arts.»
- Ch47 L4813: «He could recognize the hand of Dooku in the general's training and technique.»
- Ch47 L4813: «To the rear of the car, where Grievous's pair of MagnaGuards had made the mistake of pitting themselves against Kit Fisto, the Nautolan's blade was a cyclone of blazing blue light.»
- Ch47 L4815: «The loss of his confederates was noted by whatever computers were slaved to Grievous's organic brain, but the loss neither distracted nor slowed him.»
- Ch47 L4815: «Successful at analyzing Mace's lightsaber style, those same computers suggested that Grievous alter his stance and posture, along with the angle of his parries, ripostes, and thrusts.»
- Ch47 L4817: «Crouching low, he angled the blade downward and slashed, guiding it through the roof of the car, perpendicular to Grievous's stalwart advance.»
- Ch47 L4817: «Mace saw by the surprised look in the cyborg's reptilian eyes that, for all his strength, dexterity, and resolve, the living part of him wasn't always in perfect sync with his alloy servos.»
- Ch47 L4819: «Clearly, Grievous - - onetime courageous commander of sentient troops - - realized what Mace had done and wanted to sidestep, where General Grievous - - current commander of droids and other war machines - - wanted nothing more than to impale Mace with lunging thrusts of the paired blades.»
- Ch47 L4819: «Slipping into the gap made by Mace's saber, Grievous's left talon lost magnetic purchase on the roof, and the general faltered.»
- Ch47 L4819: «Mace came out of his crouch prepared to drive his sword into Grievous's guts, but some last-instant firing of the general's cybersynapses compelled the cyborg's torso through a swift half twist that would have sent Mace's head hurtling into the canyon had the maneuver prevailed.»
- Ch47 L4821: «Instead Mace leapt backward, out of the range of the slicing blades, and Force-pushed outward, just at the instant of Grievous's single misstep.»
- Ch47 L4821: «Off the side of the car the general went, twisting and turning as he fell, Mace trying to track the general's contorted plunge, but unsuccessfully.»
- Ch49 L4871: «Mace recognized the more scorched of the pair of gunships as one of two that had been in pursuit of Grievous's gunboat.»
- Ch49 L4875: «"Did Grievous fall from the mag-lev?"»
- Ch49 L4877: «Saw himself Force-pushing Grievous from the roof of the car; saw Grievous plunging over the edge, down out of sight, toward the rail or the canyon floor.»
- Ch49 L4887: «Moments earlier, the gunship carrying Mace and Kit had banked north, back toward the Senate District, in apparent pursuit of Grievous's gunboat.»
- Ch50 L4905: «Grievous's gunboat raced ahead, darting left and right as it fired continuously at its pursuer.»
- Ch50 L4919: «Neither Grievous nor any of his elite guards were anywhere to be found.»
- Ch50 L4921: «"I can accept that Grievous might have fallen from the mag-lev," Mace said, "but not that he would have included only two of his elite on a mission like this."»
- Ch50 L4927: «Grievous and six MagnaGuards cut a bloody swath through the broad corridors that led ultimately to Palpatine's sanctuary.»
- Ch50 L4927: «Republic soldiers - - cloned and otherwise - - fell to Grievous's lightsabers and the deadly staffs of his elite.»
- Ch50 L4927: «If nothing else, Grievous told himself, the clash would tie up two of the Jedi and dozens of troopers.»
- Ch50 L4929: «At Palpatine's apartment, Grievous had managed to fool everyone by placing the gunboat on display, then clandestinely transferring himself and his combat droids into the Republic gunship Lord Tyranus had promised would be waiting for them.»
- Ch50 L4933: «Grievous and five remaining droids completed their march to the bunker, deflecting the fire of three troopers guarding the entrance, then decapitating them.»
- Ch50 L4933: «Grievous was well aware that his lightsabers were capable of burning through the door.»
- Ch50 L4937: «The astonishment registered by Palpatine and his quartet of Jedi Knights assured Grievous that he could not have made a more dramatic entry.»
- Ch50 L4939: «Posing for effect in the polygonal opening, Grievous granted his opponents a moment to activate their lightsabers, force pikes, and other weapons.»
- Ch50 L4939: «Compared to Mace Windu, the four were mere novices, whose lightsaber techniques were some of the earliest Grievous had mastered.»
- Ch50 L4943: «Fencing with his four adversaries - - for that's all the fight amounted to - - Grievous saw six of the soldiers and three of the Red Guards jolted to spasming deaths by the MagnaGuards' double-tipped scepters.»
- Ch50 L4945: «Grievous enjoyed going against so many Jedi simultaneously.»
- Ch50 L4947: «Stepping back to absorb what had happened, the two remaining Jedi came at Grievous in concert, twirling and leaping about as if putting on some sort of crowd-pleasing martial arts demonstration.»
- Ch50 L4947: «For practice, Grievous called two more blades from his belt, grasping them in his feet even as the antigrav repulsors built into his legs were lifting him from the floor, making him every bit as agile as the Force did the Jedi.»
- Ch50 L4955: «And all for what? Grievous asked himself. The timid old man backed to the bunker's rear wall? The would-be champion of democracy, who had loosed his clone army against the merchants and builders and traders who opposed his rule - - his Republic? Best to put the Jedi out of her misery, Grievous thought.»
- Ch50 L4957: «A move Grievous could appreciate, although he was no longer in the space through which the weapon sliced.»
- Ch50 L4959: «With a gesture, Grievous instructed his elite to secure the bunker's hexagonal door.»
- Ch50 L4963: «Palpatine neither cowered nor protested. He merely said: "You will be a true loss to the forces you represent." The remark took Grievous by surprise. Was this praise?»
- Ch50 L4967: «Grievous didn't respond immediately. Was Palpatine trying to trick him?»
- Ch50 L4973: «Grievous returned the look. "You're more defiant than I was led to believe, Chancellor. But, yes, the plan will succeed - - and to your deficit. I would gladly kill you now but for my orders."»
- Ch50 L4975: «"So you take orders," Palpatine said, moving with deliberate lethargy. "Which of us, then, is the lesser?" Before Grievous could reply, he added: "My death won't end this war, General."»
- Ch50 L4977: «Grievous had wondered about that.»
- Ch50 L4979: «Grievous hurried to the bunker's communication console.»
- Ch50 L4979: «After entering the code Tyranus had provided, Grievous pressed his alloy hand to the switch.»
- Ch50 L4983: «Grievous glared at him. "Only if they fail to challenge me."»
- Ch51 L4989: «An ARC commander verified that the hijacked gunship had been piloted by droids, but the same ARC would neither confirm nor deny that Grievous had been aboard the destroyed ship. That, alone, was cause for concern. Mace and Kit thought they knew what had occurred, and hoped they were wrong.»
- Ch51 L4993: «Steps into the tunnel, their worst fears were realized when they saw three troopers hauling away a MagnaGuard, holed by more blaster bolts than would have been needed to demolish a police skimmer. The hijacked gunship had rescued Grievous after his fall from the mag-lev, Mace told himself.»
- Ch51 L4995: «But had the fall been deliberate - - part of an increasingly elaborate ruse - - or had Grievous originally planned to abduct Palpatine from the train?»
- Ch51 L4995: «Either way, how had the cyborg general known how many of his forces to commit to such a daring plan?»
- Ch51 L4999: «Only Grievous could be held accountable for what had been done to them.»
- Ch51 L4999: «Grievous had taken the blades with which the Jedi had fought.»
- Ch51 L5005: «"I fought Grievous on Hypori," she said weakly. "I knew what he was capable of. But this... And taking Palpatine..."»
- Ch51 L5009: «"The Senate may not see it that way, Mace." Shaak Ti composed herself and gazed around. "Grievous had help. Help from someone close to the top."»
- Ch51 L5015: «"I can show you," Shaak Ti said. Turning, she activated a security recording that had captured Grievous and several of his humanoid guards dragging Palpatine to the south landing pad, butchering the handful of troopers posted there, scrambling into a waiting tri-winged shuttle, lifting off into sunset clouds...»
- Ch53 L5117: «Grievous had a good lead on them.»
- Ch53 L5117: «Having access to high-clearance codes, Grievous could have plotted a proprietary launch vector for the shuttle.»
- Ch53 L5119: «The tri-wing benefited even more from the fact that Grievous was almost as adept at handling a ship as he was a lightsaber.»
- Ch53 L5121: «Grievous would lead them on spiraling chases through the thick traffic, inserting the shuttle between ships, initiating collisions, resorting to firing the shuttle's meager weapons when necessary.»
- Ch53 L5121: «Recalled from the battle outside the well, Agen Kolar, Saesee Tiin, and Pablo-Jill had come closest to incapacitating the shuttle, but, twice now, Grievous had managed to evade them by bringing the shuttle's laser cannons to bear on cargo pods and strewing local space with debris.»
- Ch53 L5123: «Grievous was unable to match them maneuver for maneuver, but his response to the attacks was to target any innocent in his sights, ultimately forcing the Jedi to fall back once again.»
- Ch53 L5125: «Except for the shuttle, which accelerated straight for Grievous 's flagship.»
- Ch54 L5149: «Grievous held to his treacherous course, even while contingency plans formed in his mind.»
- Ch54 L5149: «Many of Grievous's impassioned pursuers were so busy defending themselves that they no longer represented a threat, but three starfighters had managed to stay with the shuttle, and were continuing to harry it with surgical fire.»
- Ch54 L5149: «Uncertain as to where Grievous had stashed Palpatine, the pilots of the trio of starfighters were being careful with their bolts, but every hit was inflicting further damage to the stabilizers and shield generator.»
- Ch54 L5151: «Plasma fire from the Invisible Hand's point-defense weapons had only prompted them to close ranks with the shuttle, using it in the same way Grievous was using Palpatine - - as a kind of screen. The mechanical voice of a control droid aboard the cruiser issued from the shuttle's cockpit speakers.»
- Ch54 L5155: «"Negative," Grievous said. "Save them for when we actually need them. Continue cannon fire."»
- Ch54 L5159: «Grievous didn't doubt it.»
- Ch54 L5163: «Grievous swiveled his seat toward Palpatine, who was strapped into an acceleration couch between two MagnaGuards.»
- Ch54 L5165: «The Supreme Chancellor had been unexpectedly compliant since leaving the bunker, at times brazen enough to take Grievous to task for his less-than-perfect piloting skills.»
- Ch54 L5167: «Grievous had asked himself.»
- Ch54 L5167: «Once more, Grievous questioned the needless complexity of the Sith Lords' plan.»
- Ch54 L5169: «"Strap in, Chancellor," Grievous said now. "This could get rough."»
- Ch54 L5173: «No sooner did Grievous swing back to the viewport than gouts of fire spewed from the Invisible Hand's forward cannons.»
- Ch54 L5175: «Grievous considered ordering that the docking bay be purged of atmosphere.»
- Ch54 L5175: «Grievous would just have to deal with the starfighter pilots when the ships were released from the beam.»
- Ch54 L5177: «Before the shuttle had even settled to the deck, one of the Jedi had plunged his glowing blue blade straight through the starboard hatch. Hurrying aft through thickening smoke, Grievous caught sight of Palpatine's expression of derision.»
- Ch54 L5179: «Grievous halted just long enough to say: "We'll see who's surprised.»
- Ch54 L5181: «Battle droids lowered their weapons for fear of hitting Grievous.»
- Ch54 L5181: «Grievous waited until the last instant to command his legs to raise him up several centimeters.»
- Ch54 L5183: «Slipping past the flashing strikes of his opponents, Grievous's blades pierced the chests of both. They fell away from him, faces contorted in surprise, of the sort only sudden death could bring. Several battle droids hastened forward, almost prancing in eagerness.»
- Ch54 L5185: «"Jettison the bodies," Grievous instructed. "Choose a place where the Republic can have a good look at them." Diminutive between two MagnaGuards, Palpatine was waiting at the foot of the shuttle boarding ramp. "Take him," Grievous said.»
- Ch54 L5187: «Lifting Palpatine by his armpits, the combat droids followed Grievous through the cruiser, and at last through an oval of opalescent portal into a large cabin space containing a situation table surrounded by chairs.»
- Ch54 L5187: «Grievous ordered the guards to set the Supreme Chancellor down in a swivel chair at the head of the table and to shackle his hands.»
- Ch54 L5187: «"Welcome to the general's quarters," he said while he did input at a console built into the table.»
- Ch54 L5189: «"You're about to make an unscheduled appearance on the HoloNet, Chancellor," Grievous said. "I apologize for not providing a mirror, hairbrush, and cosmetics, so that you might at least camouflage some of your fear."»
- Ch54 L5193: «Grievous nodded at what seemed an obvious statement. "I'll display you, but you won't speak. Is that understood?"»
- Ch54 L5199: «"Very good." For no apparent reason, Grievous felt uncertain. "Lord Tyranus will soon be here to take charge of you."»
- Ch54 L5203: «From aboard his cruiser, General Grievous addressed a captive audience of trillions of beings.»
- Ch54 L5207: «"What if you're wrong, Bail? What if they do kill him, and power falls into the hands of Mas Amedda and the rest of that gang? That doesn't worry you? What if Alderaan is next on Grievous's list of worlds to attack?"»
- Ch54 L5219: «On Utapau, an Outer Rim world of vast sinkholes and lizard mounts, Viceroy Nute Gunray watched a grainy HoloNet image of General Grievous lower the boom on Coruscant.»
- Ch54 L5219: «Had he been wrong to underestimate the cyborg?»
- Ch54 L5225: «"Kill the Supreme Chancellor, Grievous will."»
- Ch54 L5237: «"To the Force, look for guidance. Accept what fate has placed before us. For now, prevent Grievous's fleet from escaping to hyperspace, you must. Recalled, many Jedi and others have been. Turn, the battle will, when they arrive."»
- Ch54 L5243: «"We'll pin Grievous here, like the vermin he is."»
- Ch55 L5249: «The sloop's Geonosian technology would mask the fact that he had jumped almost immediately to Coruscant to join Grievous, and to play out the final act of the drama Sidious had composed.»
- Ch55 L5257: «"The depth of his anger. His willingness to go beyond the Force, as the Jedi know it, and to call on the power of the dark side. General Grievous will activate a special beacon that will call Skywalker and Kenobi back to Coruscant, and onto the stage we will set for them."»

### B.2 Dooku / Tyranus: 273 entries. Aliases: `Dooku|Tyranus|the Count\b`. Version: Dooku, 19 BBY (Jedi-era/Geonosis lines are backstory)

- Ch1 L39: «His face scarred from a contest with Dooku-trained Asajj Ventress.»
- Ch4 L237: «Even if he could put all that aside, as Obi-Wan plainly wanted him to do, it was personal because Gunray had aligned himself with Dooku and the Separatists, and the war they had planned from the start had brought ruin to a thousand worlds.»
- Ch4 L241: «Twice he might have been able to kill Dooku himself had Obi-Wan not held him back.»
- Ch5 L265: «It must have been difficult for him to have served under a methodical master such as Dooku, consummate planner, consummate duelist.»
- Ch5 L285: «He recalled, as if yesterday, Dooku's taunt on Geonosis that Qui-Gon would have joined Dooku in championing the Separatist cause.»
- Ch8 L407: «"Count Dooku will help us find new worlds to settle when the war is won."»
- Ch8 L419: «It was during the final trial that Gunray had been approached by Count Dooku, who had promised that all would ultimately turn out well for the Trade Federation.»
- Ch8 L419: «Dooku has listened attentively; had promised to bring the matter to the attention of the Jedi Council, though he himself had left the Order some years earlier.»
- Ch8 L421: «Gunray had mixed feelings about Dooku's purpose in creating a Separatist movement, chiefly because corruption in the Republic Senate had so often worked to the Trade Federation's advantage.»
- Ch8 L421: «But if Dooku's Confederacy of Independent Systems could eliminate even some of the bribes and kickbacks commonplace in galactic trade, then so much the better.»
- Ch8 L421: «By and by Dooku's real aims had been made clear: he was less interested in providing an alternative to the Republic than he was in bringing the Republic to its knees - - through the use of force if necessary.»
- Ch8 L421: «In much the same way that the Trade Federation had amassed an army right under the nose of Supreme Chancellor Finis Valorum, Dooku - - in plain sight - - was seeing to it that Baktoid Armor Workshops was supplying weapons to any corporations that agreed to ally with him.»
- Ch8 L423: «Playing a game of his own, teasing Dooku along, he had informed Dooku that a precondition to their entering into any exclusive arrangement was the death of former Naboo Queen Padme Amidala, who had foiled Gunray on two occasions, and had been the loudest opposition voice at his trials.»
- Ch8 L423: «Dooku had hired a bounty hunter to oversee the business, but two attempts at assassinating Senator Amidala had failed.»
- Ch8 L423: «But just when Gunray finally had Amidala in his grasp - - on trial, no less, for espionage - - Dooku had equivocated, refusing to have Amidala killed outright, and not lifting a hand against the Jedi until some two hundred of them had showed up with a clone army the Republic had grown in secret!»
- Ch8 L425: «Hurrying to the catacombs with Dooku at their side, Gunray and Haako had barely managed to flee the embattled surface and recall what core ships and droid carriers remained.»
- Ch8 L425: «By then, though, it was too late for anyone to resign from Dooku's Confederacy.»
- Ch8 L425: «The war was begun, and it was Dooku's turn for revelations: he, too, was Sith, and his Master was none other than Sidious!»
- Ch8 L425: «For a time it appeared that Sidious and Dooku's dreams of toppling the Republic might succeed after all.»
- Ch8 L455: «"Count Dooku, you mean."»
- Ch8 L459: «Supreme Commander of the droid armies, General Grievous had been San Hill and Poggle the Lesser's gift to Dooku. Once merely a barbaric living being; now a cyborg monstrosity, devoted to death and destruction. Already the butcher of entire populations; the devastator of countless worlds...»
- Ch10 L605: «Back then, many Senators felt that the Republic should bide its time and simply allow Count Dooku's movement to play itself out. But not after the full extent of the Separatist threat became clear.»
- Ch10 L609: «Not after heavily armed corporations such as the Commerce Guild and the Techno Union had partnered with Dooku.»
- Ch10 L613: «Finally he attempted to arrange a peace summit, but Count Dooku had refused to attend.»
- Ch11 L673: «Reaching the end of the long corridor, Obi-Wan and Yoda turned and started back. Without taking his eyes from the floor, Yoda said: "Found something important, you have, Obi-Wan. That Count Dooku is in league with someone, proof this is. That in this war a greater part the Sith play than we realize."»
- Ch11 L675: «The name Sidious had come up only once since the war began - - on Geonosis, when Dooku had told an imprisoned Obi-Wan that a Sith Lord by that name had hundreds of Republic Senators under his influence.»
- Ch11 L675: «At the time, Obi-Wan assumed that Dooku was lying, in order to persuade Obi-Wan that he was still aligned with the Jedi, although attempting to thwart the powers of the dark side by his own methods.»
- Ch11 L675: «And yet, even after Dooku had revealed himself to be Sith-trained, Yoda and others on the Council continued to believe that he had been lying about Sidious.»
- Ch11 L675: «Two Council members were convinced that Dooku was the Dark Lord, having somehow tutored himself - - by Sith Holocron, perhaps - - in the use of dark side powers.»
- Ch11 L675: «A hunt for Dooku's Sith allies had been going on almost since the start of the war.»
- Ch11 L675: «Dooku was known to have trained Jedi in the dark arts - - Jedi Knights who had lost faith in the ideals of the Republic, Padawans fascinated by the power of the dark side, misinformed novices such as Asajj Ventress, who had been mentored by a Jedi - - but the question remained, who, if anyone, had been Dooku's teacher?»
- Ch11 L679: «But the horned and tattooed Sith whom Obi-Wan killed could not have trained Dooku, because Dooku had still been a member of the Jedi Order then. As clouded as the dark side made some things, there was simply no way Dooku could have been living a double life within the walls of the Temple itself.»
- Ch11 L681: «"Master Yoda," Obi-Wan said, "is it possible that Dooku wasn't lying about the Senate being under the control of Sidious?"»
- Ch11 L683: «Yoda paused for a moment, then added: "Perhaps at Geonosis, an accident it was that Dooku revealed himself.»
- Ch11 L683: «Had he not, searched we would have for Sidious, leaving Dooku to escalate his war.»
- Ch11 L685: «"I've thought long and hard about that day, Master, and I believe Dooku couldn't help revealing himself - - even though he may have regretted it.»
- Ch11 L693: «Obi-Wan considered it. "I've heard rumors about Dooku's early fascination with the dark side. Was there not an incident in the Temple involving a stolen Sith Holocron?"»
- Ch11 L695: «But understand, Obi-Wan, a Jedi Dooku was.»
- Ch11 L697: «Obi-Wan sat, his heart wanting to race. "A stern Master Dooku was, to Qui-Gon and others,"»
- Ch11 L703: «"Never absent they were, Obi-Wan. But stronger suddenly. Closer to the surface. Spoke much of the prophecy, Dooku did."»
- Ch11 L711: «Yoda regarded him for a long moment. "Difficult to say," he said quickly. "Maybe, yes; maybe, no. More important the shroud of the dark side is. Many, many discussions Dooku had. With me, with other members of the Council. Most of all, with Master Sifo-Dyas."»
- Ch11 L713: «But worried about Master Dooku, Sifo-Dyas was.»
- Ch11 L713: «Saw in Dooku the effect of Qui-Gon's death, Sifo-Dyas did.»
- Ch11 L713: «"Knew of Dooku's imminent departure, Master Sifo-Dyas did.»
- Ch11 L715: «"And yet the Council dismissed Dooku as an idealist," Obi-Wan said.»
- Ch11 L719: «"But how could Dooku have searched out Sidious? Or was it the other way around?"»
- Ch11 L721: «"Impossible to know. But accept Sidious as a mentor Dooku did."»
- Ch11 L725: «"Also impossible to know. Believed he might have, that Sidious Dooku would hunt down. To destroy."»
- Ch11 L727: «"Could that have motivated Dooku to leave the Order?"»
- Ch11 L745: «That Sifo-Dyas the order placed; that Tyranus the donor clone furnished.»
- Ch11 L745: «Seen by the Kaminoans, neither Sifo-Dyas nor Tyranus was.»
- Ch11 L749: «"From Tyranus."»
- Ch11 L751: «"Could Tyranus have been an alias for Sifo-Dyas? Could he have adopted the name to provide deniability for the Jedi in case the clone army was discovered?"»
- Ch11 L757: «"Someone knew," Obi-Wan said, more to himself. "Dooku?" he asked Yoda.»
- Ch11 L759: «"A theory I have - - nothing more. Murder, Dooku committed. Then, from the Jedi archives erased Kamino, he did. Of that tampering, proof Master Jocasta Nu found - - proof of Dooku's action, though well concealed it was."»
- Ch11 L761: «What had caused him that day three years earlier to stare so intently at the library's bronzium bust of Count Dooku?»
- Ch11 L761: «"Could Sifo-Dyas and Tyranus have been partners?"»
- Ch11 L763: «"Of our ignorance, another example this is. But playing both sides Jango Fett clearly was. By someone on the side of the Republic, chosen he was on Bogg Four to be the clone template. But serving Dooku he was, as a hired killer. With the changeling who targeted Amidala, an intermediary he was."»
- Ch11 L765: «Obi-Wan pictured Fett in the execution arena on Geonosis, standing behind Dooku in a box reserved for dignitaries. "He had knowledge of both armies. Could he have killed Sifo-Dyas?"»
- Ch11 L769: «"Were you able to trace the source of the payments - - beyond Tyranus, I mean?"»
- Ch11 L777: «"So Dooku had no choice but to create an army before the clones were trained and ready."»
- Ch11 L779: «"When I was being held captive on Geonosis, Dooku told me that the Trade Federation had been allied with Sidious during the blockade of Naboo, but that they had later been betrayed by him.»
- Ch11 L779: «Dooku said that Gunray had gone to him for help, and that Dooku had tried to appeal to the Council.»
- Ch11 L781: «"More lies," Yoda said. "Building a case to enlist you in his cause, Dooku was." You must join me, Obi-Wan, Dooku had said, and together we will destroy the Sith! "If Gunray hadn't been so keen on assassinating Padme Amidala,"»
- Ch11 L795: «"In the nick of time. The Council couldn't conceive of Dooku as anything but an idealist. Perhaps he never believed that the Jedi could become generals."»
